#### Contradictions with REQUIREMENTS.md / ADRs

##### Q1 — Does `MedplumClient` need anything beyond `/oauth2/token` + `/auth/me` for client-credentials and authorization-code logins?

**Endpoints: no — only `/oauth2/authorize` (redirect target), `/oauth2/token`, `/auth/me`, `/oauth2/logout`. Response shapes: yes — three non-standard things the SDK reads.**

Evidence, client credentials:
- `startClientLogin` → `fetchTokens({grant_type:'client_credentials', client_id, client_secret})` (`packages/core/src/client.ts:4339-4348`, `4661-4699`), form-encoded POST to `this.tokenUrl` = `baseUrl + 'oauth2/token'` (`client.ts:1078`), credentials in the body or Basic header (`authCredentialsMethod:'header'`, `client.ts:1084, 4668-4675`). Nothing else is called.
- `verifyTokens` (`client.ts:4708-4738`) parses the **access token payload** and requires: `exp` in the future; and if a `cid` claim exists it must equal `clientId`, otherwise **`client_id` must equal `clientId`** (`4724-4731`) — a Keycloak user token carries `azp`, not `client_id` (service-account tokens do carry `client_id`), so a mapper is needed for authorization-code logins.
- It then stores `LoginState{accessToken, refreshToken, project: tokens.project, profile: tokens.profile}` (`client.ts:4733-4738`, types `568-583`) — **`project` and `profile` are read from the token response** (server `oauth/token.ts:963-964`). They are used for the stored-login list / account switcher (`addLogin`, `syncStoredLoginProject`, `client.ts:3240-3298`) and `checkSessionDetailsMatchLogin` (`4740-4749`); they are not needed for API calls, but `undefined` there breaks `HeaderDropdown`/`getLogins()` displays in `@medplum/app`.
- `setAccessToken` sets `this.medplumServer = isMedplumAccessToken(token)` which is **`typeof payload.login_id === 'string'`** (`client.ts:3224-3229`, `packages/core/src/jwt.ts:90-97`). `refreshProfile` returns immediately when `!this.medplumServer` (`client.ts:3246-3249`). So **without a `login_id` claim the SDK never calls `/auth/me`**, `getProfile()/getProject()/getProjectMembership()/getAccessPolicy()` stay `undefined`, and `@medplum/react`'s `useMedplumProfile` treats the user as signed-out. Big Book must emit a `login_id` claim (any string; the Keycloak session id `sid` is the natural value).
- `/auth/me` must return `SessionDetails {project, membership, profile, config, accessPolicy}` (`client.ts:902-908`); read fields: `profile.id` (`3255`), `project.name` (`3283`), `project.superAdmin` (`3310`), `membership.admin` (`3319`), and the whole `profile`, `config`, `accessPolicy` objects via getters (`3327-3381`).

Evidence, authorization code:
- `signInWithRedirect` → `requestAuthorization` builds `GET <authorizeUrl>?response_type=code&state=<pkceState>&client_id&redirect_uri=<origin>&code_challenge_method=S256&code_challenge&scope=openid profile` (`client.ts:1614-1622`, `4192-4205`); on return `processCode` posts `grant_type=authorization_code, code, client_id, redirect_uri, code_verifier` (`4213-4227`) and runs the same `verifyTokens` path. `state` is never checked. Refresh uses `grant_type=refresh_token` with `client_id` only (`4299-4303`), i.e. a public client. Logout posts `{}` with a bearer to `oauth2/logout` (`1601-1604`). No discovery document, no JWKS fetch, no `/auth/*` call other than `/auth/me`.

Consequence for BB-R-004 §8 / ADR-003 "`/oauth2/authorize|token|userinfo` … proxied to Keycloak unchanged": true for v0.1 (curl-level, BB-R-014 exit test v0.1 — the `curl` samples in `docs/api/oauth/token.md:75-130` send Basic + `grant_type=client_credentials`/`authorization_code`, which Keycloak accepts as-is). **False for SDK-grade C (v0.2)**: `/oauth2/token` must be *wrapped* to (a) add `project` and `profile` to the JSON body and (b) guarantee `login_id` + `client_id` claims in the access token (mappers). Recommend rewording BB-R-004 §8 to "`/oauth2/authorize`, `/oauth2/userinfo`, `/.well-known/jwks.json` proxied; `/oauth2/token` proxied with response enrichment (`project`, `profile`); claims via mappers", and adding "`login_id`, `client_id`" to §9's claim list. BB-R-004 §9 currently says the token carries `project`, `profile`, `membership` claims; Medplum's token carries **`profile`** but neither `project` nor `membership` (MW-007) — Big Book's extra claims are harmless, but the SDK-visible contract is `login_id`/`client_id`/`exp`/`profile`.

##### Q2 — Does `@medplum/app` have any config path to sign in via a plain OIDC redirect without `Login` / `/auth/login`?

**No.**
- `packages/app/src/config.ts:3-10` — `MedplumAppConfig` is exactly `{baseUrl, clientId, googleClientId, recaptchaSiteKey, registerEnabled, awsTextractEnabled}`; `.env.defaults` adds nothing else.
- `packages/app/src/index.tsx:20-31` constructs `MedplumClient({baseUrl, clientId, storagePrefix, cacheTime, autoBatchTime, onUnauthenticated → '/signin?next='})` — no `authorizeUrl`/`tokenUrl` override and no call to `signInWithRedirect`; a grep of `packages/app/src` for `signInWithRedirect|signInWithExternalAuth|processCode|oauth2` returns only `OAuthPage.tsx` (which *is* the Medplum authorization UI, not a consumer of one).
- `packages/app/src/SignInPage.tsx:30-46` renders `<SignInForm>` from `@medplum/react`; `SignInForm` (`packages/react/src/auth/SignInForm.tsx:165-238`) is a state machine over `LoginAuthenticationResponse` that calls `POST auth/method` (`AuthenticationForm.tsx:80`), `startLogin` → `POST auth/login` (`AuthenticationForm.tsx:170`), `startGoogleLogin` → `auth/google` (`:91`), `auth/mfa/login-enroll|verify|send-email` (`SignInForm.tsx:131-212`), `auth/profile` (`ChooseProfileForm.tsx:57`), `auth/scope` (`ChooseScopeForm.tsx:41`), `auth/newproject`, and `GET auth/login/:id` (`SignInForm.tsx:159`) before finally `processCode(code)` (`SignInForm.tsx:74`). Every branch requires a `Login` id.
- The only external-IdP path in the app is `/auth/method` returning `authorizeUrl` (`AuthenticationForm.tsx:60-77`), which redirects to the IdP with `redirect_uri=<server>/auth/external`; the server callback (`auth/external.ts:156-175`) still creates a `Login` and sends the browser to `<app>/signin?login=<id>`, which `SignInForm` resolves through `GET /auth/login/:id`. So even brokered sign-in depends on `Login`, `/auth/external` and `/auth/login/:id`.
- `/oauth2/authorize` itself never renders UI — it redirects to `<appBaseUrl>oauth` (`oauth/authorize.ts:312-318`) and `OAuthPage.tsx:57-66` completes the flow by redirecting `code` to the client. The app is therefore *the* login UI, which is why it cannot be pointed at Keycloak's login page.

Conclusion: the v1.0 line in REQUIREMENTS.md ("blocked on emulating the `Login` sign-in flow; Claude Code to confirm `@medplum/app` has no `signInWithRedirect` config path") is **confirmed — there is no such path; app-grade C stays at v1.0.** Minimum emulation for app-grade C would be: `POST /auth/login`, `GET /auth/login/:id`, `POST /auth/profile`, `POST /auth/scope`, `POST /auth/method` (may return `{}`), `GET /auth/clientinfo/:id` (may return `{}`), and the `code` → `/oauth2/token` handoff, plus MFA routes if `mfaRequired` is used — all backed by Keycloak Direct Access Grants (password grant) with a Big Book-side pending-login table; this is what ADR-003 defers.

##### Other contradictions / clarifications

1. **BB-R-004 §2 "session listing/revocation — Keycloak"**: in Medplum these are `/auth/me.security.sessions` (AUTH-009) and `POST /auth/revoke` (AUTH-017), consumed by `packages/app/src/SecurityPage.tsx:38-50`. The SDK does not read `security` (`SessionDetails` has no such field), so v0.1 may omit it; app-grade needs it (v1.0).
2. **BB-R-004 §5 "Direct external auth — defer v0.2"**: Medplum's direct external auth (MW-004) is "accept a JWT from a configured issuer as bearer". With Keycloak as the only issuer this is Big Book's *baseline* (MW-001), not a deferral; what is deferred is accepting *additional* issuers (Auth0 etc.) directly, which Keycloak cannot do for a resource server. Suggest rewording §5 to "third-party issuers accepted directly (Medplum `externalAuthProviders`) — defer v0.2".
3. **BB-R-004 §8 "`/oauth2/logout` re-shaped"**: correct — Medplum's logout is a bearer-authenticated POST returning an `OperationOutcome` (OA-010) with no OIDC `end_session` semantics; the SDK's `signOut` depends on that shape (`client.ts:1601-1604`).
4. **BB-R-004 §8 "`/.well-known/openid-configuration` re-shaped"**: correct, but note Medplum advertises `registration_endpoint`/`introspection_endpoint` and no `end_session_endpoint`; nothing in the SDK reads discovery (`client.ts:1077-1079` hard-codes paths), so only external conformance tooling cares.
5. **BB-R-004 §3 "MFA (TOTP) per user"**: Medplum also has **email OTP** (`allowedMfaMethods=email`, AUTH-021/024/025) and a **project-wide** `mfaRequired` setting in addition to the per-user invite flag; and MFA is bypassed for Google/external/exchange logins (MW-018). Keycloak has no native email-OTP authenticator (extension) — list as a known gap.
6. **BB-R-004 §2 "client assertion (private_key_jwt)"**: Medplum's assertion is verified against `ClientApplication.jwksUri` with `aud` = token URL and `iss===sub` (OA-007) — Keycloak "Signed JWT" client authenticator with a JWKS URL matches. But the SDK's `startJwtBearerLogin` (`grant_type=jwt-bearer`) is **not** a Medplum server feature (OA-021); do not list it as a compat target.
7. **BB-R-004 §8a lists** `/auth/login|newuser|newproject|newpatient|profile|scope|mfa/*|changepassword|resetpassword|setpassword|verifyemail`; the real non-goal set is larger: also `/auth/method`, `/auth/login/:id`, `/auth/google`, `/auth/external`, `/auth/exchange`, `/auth/revoke`, `/auth/preauthorize`, `/auth/clientinfo/:id`, `/oauth2/introspect`, `/oauth2/register` (see table). `/auth/revoke` and `/auth/preauthorize` are deferrals rather than non-goals.
8. **BB-R-014 §3 "`Login` non-goal"** is consistent; note that `UserSecurityRequest`, `DomainConfiguration`, `JsonWebKey` and `SmartAppLaunch` are further Medplum admin resource types touched by auth that BB-R-014 §3 does not mention — `SmartAppLaunch` matters for BB-R-022.
9. **ADR-003 "everything else proxied to Keycloak unchanged"** vs `/oauth2/userinfo`: Medplum returns `sub`=`User.id` and `profile`="Practitioner/<id>" (OA-009); Keycloak's `profile` claim is a URL per OIDC. Only `medplum-client-external-idp-demo` reads userinfo; acceptable drift, but a `fhirUser` mapper is cheap.
10. **BB-R-004 exit test** "a user invited with `mfaRequired` cannot complete login without TOTP" matches Medplum only for password logins (MW-018).
11. **Rate limits**: Medplum's are per-IP path categories with a `RateLimit` header (MW-014); BB-R-023 (Traefik) is fine, but `/auth/me` must not fall in the auth bucket if the SDK's every-token-refresh `/auth/me` call is to stay cheap.

#### Notes for the Java port

**Token claims the SDK actually reads** (`packages/core/src/client.ts`): `exp` (`4716`, `3227` via `tryGetJwtExpiration`), `cid` / `client_id` (`4721-4731`), `login_id` (`packages/core/src/jwt.ts:93` → gates `/auth/me`, `client.ts:3247`). Everything else in MW-007 (`sub`, `username`, `scope`, `profile`, `email`, `aud`) is server-internal or for third-party SMART apps. Recommended Keycloak mappers: `login_id` ← `sid` (hardcoded/"session id" mapper or a tiny script mapper), `client_id` ← `azp` for user tokens (service-account tokens already carry `client_id`), `profile` ← per-organisation user attribute (e.g. `bigbook.profile.<orgAlias>`), and `organization` (native in Keycloak 26 with `organization` scope) as the project key. Because Medplum's `profile` differs per project, a token must be bound to one organisation — use the `organization:<alias>` dynamic scope or an org-selection step in the BB `/auth/me` glue.

**Token response fields the SDK stores**: `access_token`, `refresh_token`, `project: {reference:'Project/<id>', display?}`, `profile: {reference:'Practitioner/<id>', display?}` (`client.ts:4733-4738`, `568-583`). `expires_in`, `id_token`, `scope`, `token_type` are ignored. SMART fields (`patient`, `encounter`, `fhirContext`, `smart_style_url`, `need_patient_banner`, `hub.*`) are read by SMART apps only → BB-R-022.

**`/auth/me` fields** (AUTH-009): the SDK needs `profile` (full resource with `id`), `project.{id,name,superAdmin}` (+ `features`, `strictMode` used by the app), `membership.{id,admin,profile,user}`, `config` (`UserConfiguration` with `menu` — the app renders it; default menu generator in `auth/me.ts:135-171` is worth copying verbatim), `accessPolicy` (compiled effective policy — the React `AccessPolicy`-aware components hide fields from it). `user` and `security` are app-only (Security page). The response passes through attachment URL rewriting (presigned URLs, BB-R-009 §3).

**Cookie behaviour**: the only cookie is `medplum-<clientId>` (MW-010), set by the API on login responses (`SameSite=None; Secure; HttpOnly; Max-Age=3600`) and read only by `/oauth2/authorize` for silent re-auth. No API call is cookie-authenticated. Big Book needs none of this; Keycloak's `KEYCLOAK_SESSION`/`AUTH_SESSION_ID` cookies cover SSO and `prompt=none`. CORS must still allow credentials for the SDK's `credentials:'include'` token call (harmless).

**Refresh semantics to reproduce**: public-client refresh (`client_id` only) with rotation; the SDK retries a 401 once with a forced refresh and then calls `onUnauthenticated` (`client.ts:4147-4157`); default `refreshGracePeriod` 5 min drives proactive refresh (`client.ts:1087, 3208-3216`). Keycloak: mark the SDK/app client public, enable "Revoke Refresh Token", set access lifespan ≥ 5 min.

**Things Keycloak cannot do natively (glue or defer)**: (1) `project`/`profile` enrichment of the token response and `/auth/me` (glue, v0.1/v0.2); (2) HTTP Basic client credentials on resource endpoints (MW-002; glue cache for CLI compat, v0.2); (3) bearer-authenticated `/oauth2/logout` and `/auth/revoke` (glue via admin API `DELETE …/sessions/{sid}`); (4) email OTP as an MFA factor (extension); (5) HIBP breached-password check (community SPI); (6) external-to-internal token exchange is a preview feature in Keycloak 26 (`--features=token-exchange`), standard token exchange only handles internal tokens — decide in BB-R-024; (7) pre-authorized code grant for magic links (Keycloak's OID4VCI pre-authorized code is preview and credential-oriented) — glue if needed; (8) `X-Medplum-On-Behalf-Of` (impersonation token exchange with `requested_subject` needs fine-grained admin permissions; a Big Book filter honouring the header for org admins is simpler); (9) SMART `launch`/`aud`/`SmartAppLaunch` context and `patient/` scope compilation (ADR-005); (10) per-IP login rate limiting with the `RateLimit` header (Traefik); (11) IP access rules at login (Keycloak has no per-client IP allowlist authenticator natively — script/conditional authenticator); (12) `retiringSecret` dual-secret rotation (Keycloak "client secret rotation" policy covers it); (13) project-scoped issuers under `/projects/:id/` (non-goal).

**Error shapes**: `/oauth2/token` errors are OAuth JSON `{error, error_description}` (400), but several paths throw FHIR `OperationOutcome` (e.g. `patient/` scope with client credentials, IP block, 429 from upstream). Auth failures on other routes are `OperationOutcome` 401/403/400 (`Authentication error` is 400, not 401). Keycloak's token errors are already OAuth-shaped; Big Book's 401 entry point must emit `OperationOutcome` + `WWW-Authenticate` (MW-005).


---

## Area 2 — decisions needed

Same convention as area 1. **D** = design call (changes a brick or an ADR line), **T** = text fix (the brick is wrong about Medplum; no design change). Evidence in sections 2.1–2.3 below.

Note: area 2 produced a third category. **V** = *verify on pinned HAPI* — a question the code cannot answer because it depends on HAPI's behaviour, not Medplum's. These are a test list for whoever stands up the HAPI container (issue #8), not decisions for the PO. 50 rows carry the flag; the ones that matter are collected in V1–V6.

### Design calls

| # | Topic | What Medplum does | What the docs say now | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D11 | Transaction atomicity (BB-R-001.4) | A `transaction` bundle is atomic **only** when the project carries the `transaction-bundles` feature. Without it the bundle runs as a batch, silently, and still returns `transaction-response`. Capped at 50 updates, and at 8 entries when any conditional operation is present. | Brick reads as though transactions are unconditionally atomic in v0.1 | Big Book: transactions **always** atomic (HAPI default), no feature gate, no caps. Record Medplum's gate and caps in `medplum-parity.md` as a deliberate divergence. The brick's exit test currently cannot detect the difference — add an entry-rollback assertion. |
| D12 | No-op update suppression (BB-R-001.1 "new version every time") | Byte-identical PUT returns the existing resource with no new version; the router surfaces 304 and `@medplum/core.upsertResource` branches on it (`client.ts:2425-2429`). HAPI versions unconditionally. | "PUT — new version every time" | Strike "every time" from BB-R-001.1. SDK-grade (v0.2) needs the 304 path as glue (~40 lines, compare before write). v0.1 may version unconditionally and say so in the parity guide. |
| D13 | Client-assigned ids inside bundles (BB-R-001.9) | Every batch/transaction entry gets `assignedId`, so any user's POST inside a bundle can carry a client-supplied id (uniqueness pre-checked). This is how `urn:uuid` references resolve. | "Server-assigned UUID ids only" | Add the sub-clause: ids may be client-assigned inside bundles to resolve `urn:uuid`, uniqueness enforced. Otherwise BB-R-001.4 and BB-R-001.9 contradict each other. |
| D14 | Access-policy filtering fails open (BB-R-006.1, ADR-001) | A malformed `criteria` makes `addAccessPolicyFilters` `return` out of the whole loop, dropping every remaining restriction for that request. Authorisation also runs **twice** per write, pre and post. | ADR-001 describes the pre-check only | Big Book fails **closed** on unparseable criteria, and ADR-001 must specify both phases (post-write check is what stops a resource being moved out of its policy's criteria). Record the divergence. |
| D15 | The in-memory matcher (BB-R-006.1, BB-R-007.1) | `matchesSearchRequest` backs `Subscription.criteria` and `AccessPolicy` write-side checks, and is wrong five ways: unknown/chained/`_has`/`_filter` codes → `false`; `number`/`quantity` → `false`; strings are case-insensitive substring where SQL is prefix, `:exact` ignored; `:not-in`/`:in`/`:above`/`:below`/`:text` degrade to equality (so **`:not-in` computes its own inverse** — a policy bypass); dates compare lexicographically. | Bricks assume criteria mean the same thing everywhere | **Validate criteria at write time and reject anything the matcher cannot evaluate.** Do not inherit silent `false`. HAPI's `InMemoryResourceMatcher` has its own gap list; enforce the intersection. Highest-severity finding in area 2. |
| D16 | Cross-project references (BB-R-005.1) | Nothing prevents *writing* a reference into another project. `checkReferencesOnWrite` is off by default and is what makes a dangling cross-project reference an error; the `projectId` predicate only stops *reads*. | "resources in one project never reference another" | Reword to "cannot *resolve* references into another project", and default `enforce_referential_integrity_on_write` **on** in Big Book — stricter than Medplum, and cheap. |
| D17 | Paging wire-compatibility (BB-R-002.7) | Cursor is `2-<epochMillis>-<csv uuids>`, not opaque as the docs claim, and engages only when four conditions hold at once (`_offset` unset, `_count>=20`, one sort rule, `_lastUpdated` ascending). HAPI's is `?_getpages=<uuid>` with server-side state. Separately Medplum **re-serialises** page links, silently dropping `_summary`/`_format`/`_pretty` — page 2 of a `_summary=true` search returns full resources. | "document the difference from Medplum's cursor" | Commit to: offset paging byte-compatible; `next`/`previous`/`self` present with correct semantics; cursor token explicitly **not** wire-compatible. Echo the caller's params in page links rather than re-serialising — fix Medplum's bug, don't port it. Exit test: blind `next`-following terminates. |
| D18 | GraphQL introspection and limits (BB-R-003.4) | Introspection is a **server-wide** config key on a lazily built singleton router, not the `graphql-introspection` project feature (that flag is read by no server code — it is dead, and Medplum's own two doc pages contradict each other). `graphqlMaxDepth` and the query-cost rule enforce **nothing**: both `reportError` calls are commented out, leaving a log line. | "togglable per project; depth/search-count limits configurable" | Reword: introspection server-wide in v0.1, per-project v0.2; depth and cost limits **enforcing** in Big Book (Medplum's are advisory). Enforcing depth is the only DoS control on `$graphql`, ~40-60 lines. |
| D19 | Quantity and number search correctness (BB-R-002.1) | Quantity search **discards system and code** and matches the number alone. Number search has no implicit precision ranges (literal `// TODO`). HAPI does both correctly. | "string/token/reference/date/quantity semantics" | Take HAPI's correct behaviour. Record in `medplum-parity.md` as "Big Book is more correct than Medplum here"; write no bug-compat glue. Note a Big Book quantity search returns **fewer** results than Medplum's. |
| D20 | `$export` and async (BB-R-020, BB-R-001.7) | `$export` at all three levels **ignores `Prefer: respond-async`** and is unconditionally async — non-conformant to the Bulk Data IG. Only five handlers in the server read that header, none of them a CRUD route. Poll URLs are Medplum-shaped (`/fhir/R4/job/:id/status`, `/fhir/R4/bulkdata/export/:id`); HAPI uses `$export-poll-status?_jobId=`. | BB-R-001.7 defers async batch to v0.2; BB-R-020 wires HAPI `$export` | Keep HAPI's conformant behaviour. For v0.2 SDK compat, alias the two Medplum poll URLs to HAPI's job status (the client follows the `Content-Location` we hand out, so a thin alias suffices). |
| D21 | FHIR quota and resource caps (no brick) | Per-interaction-weight quota (READ=1 … WRITE=100) with per-membership and per-project buckets, surfaced as a `RateLimit: "fhirInteractions"` header. Resource caps limit total resources per project. | Not mentioned anywhere | Both are SaaS controls. Resource caps → **non-goal**. Quota → non-goal in v0.x; `@medplum/core` does not require the header. BB-R-023 (Traefik) stays coarse HTTP limiting. |
| D22 | `$expunge` / hard delete (no brick) | Super-admin `$expunge` and `purgeResources` are the only ways to remove history; retention is otherwise unbounded with no pruning. | No brick mentions either | Add to BB-R-018 (v0.2, audit) as `wire HAPI $expunge`. Needed for GDPR-style erasure and for the `purge` super-admin row (area 1 D8). |

### Text fixes (area 2)

| # | Brick | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| T18 | BB-R-001.3 `_history` | Medplum has **instance-level `_history` only** — no type-level, no system-level (`GET /Patient/_history` returns 404). HAPI has all three. Reword to instance-level as the Medplum bar, note the HAPI superset. Retention is unbounded with no pruning. |
| T19 | BB-R-001.2 conditional operations | `multipleMatches` → **412** `id=multiple-matches` on conditional create, update, delete **and** patch. The brick mentions only conditional update. Pin HAPI `allow_multiple_delete=false`. Add the two conditional update-as-create cases: 0 matches with a body `id` → 400; 0 matches without → 201. |
| T20 | BB-R-001.5 `$validate` | Type-level only in Medplum (`POST /:resourceType/$validate`, validates the body). No instance-level, no GET. HAPI has both. |
| T21 | BB-R-003.1 `$graphql` paths | Only `POST /fhir/R4/$graphql` exists. No instance-level `$graphql`, no GET. Strike the instance path or mark it a HAPI superset. |
| T22 | BB-R-002.8 `_text`/`_content` | Medplum **never implemented** these — the params 400 as unknown. This is a new capability from HAPI + OpenSearch, not a deferral of parity. Medplum's nearest analogue is the token `:text` modifier over a 7-param allowlist. |
| T23 | BB-R-002.1 modifiers | Medplum parses and then **400s** on `:above`, `:below`, `:in`, `:not-in`, `:of-type`. HAPI implements them — a net gain, so the exit test must not assert Medplum's 400s. Medplum's own extensions `sw` and `:present` are non-goals. |
| T24 | BB-R-002.3 includes | `_include=*` / `_revinclude=*` unsupported in Medplum (400), supported in HAPI. `:iterate` capped at depth 5 / 1000 entries, breaching throws **500** not 400. `_revinclude` sub-searches hard-pinned to `_count=1000`, silently truncating. |
| T25 | BB-R-002.5 `_filter` | Keep `wire HAPI`, but add an exit item: measure which operators and whether dotted paths work. Medplum's `_filter` recurses into the full builder so dotted paths become chained searches; its documented `in`/`ni` ValueSet operators **throw 400** in code. |
| T26 | BB-R-002.6 compartment | Two parameters, not one: `_project` over `meta.project` and `_compartment` over `meta.compartment`. `_compartment` can also appear as a chain link. |
| T27 | BB-R-002.1 SearchParameters | Medplum loads them from **static JSON bundles at boot**; a `SearchParameter` resource POSTed to the store is inert. HAPI supports runtime registration plus `$reindex` — a free gain worth stating. |
| T28 | BB-R-014.1 status codes and content type | **422 is effectively unreachable**: only `business-rule` maps to 422, and `badRequest`/`validationError` emit no `id`, so validation lands on 400. Change to "404/410/412/400 semantics (422 only for `business-rule`)". `application/fhir+json` is not universal — `$graphql`, `/metadata`, `$versions`, well-knowns and the bulk manifest return `application/json`. |
| T29 | BB-R-014.6 v0.2 glue list | Add eight missing items: `Location` without `/_history/<vid>`; batch entry `response.status` bare `"201"` and relative `response.location`; DELETE body `OperationOutcome{id:'ok'}`; honouring `X-Medplum: extended`; method-not-allowed 404 not 405; the `Access-Control-Expose-Headers` list; `$graphql` returning `application/json`. Also: the `_count` cap 1000 lives in the search layer, not the route layer. |
| T30 | BB-R-005.7 super-admin | The code has **two** elevated identities: the super-admin *user* repo (skips project filters, keeps a policy) and the *system* repo (no projects, no policy), obtainable from any repository by calling `getSystemRepo()` with no audit trail. Big Book's system-repo equivalent must be internal, non-token-reachable, and logged. |
| T31 | BB-R-006.1 criteria restriction | "`:not`/`:missing` only, no chaining" is a **convention, not enforced** — `addAccessPolicyFilters` calls the full search builder, so a chained criteria compiles and works. Either enforce the restriction or drop the claim. |

### Verify on pinned HAPI (issue #8)

| # | Question | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|
| V1 | Does `SearchNarrowingInterceptor` fire for `_include`/`_revinclude` sub-queries, and does `GraphQLProvider` resolve through the DAO/interceptor chain? | A miss is a **cross-tenant data leak**, not a feature gap. Medplum gets both right by construction. Measure before any other BB-R-002/003 work. |
| V2 | Which `_filter` operators does HAPI support, and do dotted paths work? | Decides whether BB-R-002.5's `wire HAPI` fill is right or becomes glue/v0.2. |
| V3 | Does HAPI's GraphQL provider cover connections (`PatientConnection`) and mutations (`PatientCreate`)? | BB-R-003.2/3 already say "measure the gap; if absent, defer v0.2, not glue". |
| V4 | Are runtime-registered custom `SearchParameter` resources enabled by default, and does `$reindex` pick them up? | A free capability gain over Medplum if yes. |
| V5 | What does HAPI's `InMemoryResourceMatcher` support, versus the SQL search? | Sets the intersection that D15's write-time criteria validation must enforce. |
| V6 | HAPI's `_count` cap, default `_total` mode, and page-link shape. | Feeds BB-R-002.4/7 and the parity guide. |

---

## Area 2 — FHIR routes, repository, search (draft)

### 2.1 FHIR HTTP routes and request/response envelope


Source snapshot: `medplum/medplum` `main` @ `fbc8e7b4b` (server 5.1.x). Source paths are relative to `packages/server/src` unless prefixed `fhir-router/` (= `packages/fhir-router/src`) or `core/` (= `packages/core/src`). Docs snapshot: `docs/medplum-docs` scraped 2026-09-12. BB-R mapping against REQUIREMENTS.md at big-book `4a94f31`.

Conventions used in Behaviour: "authenticated" = passed `oauth/middleware.ts authenticateRequest`; "FhirRouter" = the shared `@medplum/fhir-router` dispatcher reached via the catch-all in `fhir/routes.ts:479`; "outcome status" = `core/outcomes.ts getStatus()` mapping of `OperationOutcome.id`. All error bodies are FHIR `OperationOutcome` served as `application/fhir+json`. Observed status codes are cited from `docs/guides/medplum-parity.md`, not re-derived.

Mount prefixes: every route below is reachable at **four** prefixes — `/fhir/R4/…`, `/api/fhir/R4/…`, `/projects/:projectId/fhir/R4/…`, `/api/projects/:projectId/fhir/R4/…` (`app.ts:267-270`). Rows quote the canonical `/fhir/R4` form.

If the storage read throws and headers are not yet sent, `ETag`/`Last-Modified`/`Content-Type` are removed and a **404** `not-found` is sent. #### 2a.2 Envelope and cross-cutting behaviour (FE-xxx)

| ID | Capability | Source (file:line) | Behaviour | Doc ref | Used by | Proposed fate | BB-R |

| FE-001 | Response body serialisation and `application/fhir+json` | `fhir/routes.ts:101-144`; `fhir/response.ts:99-100`; `core/utils.ts:609-612` | A router-level interceptor monkey-patches `res.json`. When the outgoing `Content-Type` starts with `application/fhir+json` (which `sendFhirResponse` always sets unless the handler overrode it) the body is serialised with `@medplum/core stringify`, which **recursively strips `undefined`, `null` and empty-string properties and empty objects/arrays** before `JSON.stringify`. This is Medplum's canonical FHIR JSON. When no `Content-Type` has been set the interceptor defaults to `application/json` and uses plain `JSON.stringify`. A project `systemSetting` named `legacyFhirJsonResponseFormat: true` opts back into plain `JSON.stringify` for fhir+json too. | `none` | every FHIR response | glue — Jackson `NON_EMPTY` inclusion plus an empty-collection filter; HAPI's parser already omits nulls but keeps empty arrays | BB-R-014.1 |
| FE-002 | `_pretty=true` query parameter | `fhir/routes.ts:124,138,143` | Any FHIR response with `?_pretty=true` (exact string match on the query value) is serialised with 2-space indentation. Applies to both the `stringify` and the plain-JSON paths. Not a FHIR-standard parameter. Undocumented. | `none` | debugging / curl | glue — 5 lines, or `defer v0.2`; not needed for SDK compat | GAP |
| FE-003 | **No `_format` support** | (absent from `packages/server/src`) | Medplum ignores the FHIR-standard `_format` query parameter entirely — a grep of the server source finds no reference. Content negotiation is by the `Accept` header only, and only for `Binary` (FR-036). XML is not supported at all (`CapabilityStatement.format: ['json']`). | `none` | — | glue — HAPI honours `_format` and serves XML by default; **must disable XML** (`FhirContext` narrowing / `ResponseHighlighterInterceptor` off) to match | BB-R-014.1 |
| FE-004 | `Prefer: return=minimal` | `fhir/routes.ts:115-118` | If the request's `Prefer` header is **exactly** the string `return=minimal` (no parameter parsing, no `handling=` support, case-sensitive on the value) the interceptor calls `res.send()` with no argument — an **empty body** with the status and headers already set by `sendResponseHeaders`. `return=representation` and `return=OperationOutcome` are named in the comment but **not implemented**: both fall through to the default full-resource body. | `none` | FHIR clients | wire HAPI — HAPI's `PreferHeader` handles minimal/representation/OperationOutcome properly; Medplum's is a subset, so HAPI is a superset (safe) | BB-R-014.1 |

If it is: the project must have the **`async-batch` feature** else **400** `Async Batch feature not available`; the body is then parsed with a **separate `json()` limited to `maxBatchSize` (default `50mb`)** rather than `maxJsonSize` (default `1mb`); a non-`Bundle` body → **400** `Expected request body to be a Bundle`; a `transaction` bundle **when `transaction-bundles` is also enabled** → **400** `Transaction batches cannot be executed asynchronously` with `expression: [Bundle.type]`. Plain create/read/update/delete/search/GraphQL silently ignore the header. | `fhir-datastore/processing-async-bundles.md — Build > FHIR Datastore > FHIR Data > Processing Asynchronous Bundles` (bundles only) | `MedplumClient.startAsyncRequest()` (`core/client.ts:3570`); app `admin/SuperAdminStartAsyncJob.tsx:37` | defer v0.2 | BB-R-020 (v0.2), BB-R-016 (v0.2) |
| FE-007 | `ETag` header | `fhir/response.ts:23-35`; `app.ts:196` | `app.set('etag', false)` disables Express's own ETag entirely. `sendResponseHeaders` sets `ETag: W/"<meta.versionId>"` whenever the response body carries `meta.versionId` — on read, vread, create, update, patch and the Binary routes. It is **not** set on the single-element outcome path (delete, `$validate`, `$reindex`, `$resend`) nor on search bundles (a Bundle has no `meta.versionId`). Passed as `options.ifMatch` to `updateResource`, `conditionalUpdate`, `patchResource` and `conditionalPatch` — **not** to `deleteResource` or `conditionalDelete` (Medplum has no conditional-delete version check). A mismatch raises `preconditionFailed` → **412**, `id=precondition-failed`, `issue.code=processing`, "Precondition Failed". In a bundle, a conditional create additionally forces `requiresStrongTransaction` (serializable isolation) and, at preprocess time, resolves to the **existing** resource's reference when exactly one match is found, so other entries' `urn:uuid` references point at the pre-existing resource rather than creating a duplicate. | `fhir-datastore/fhir-batch-requests.md — Build > FHIR Datastore > FHIR Data > FHIR Batch Requests` | `MedplumClient.createResourceIfNoneExist()` | wire HAPI — native; glue for the 412 slug | BB-R-001.2, BB-R-001.4 |

The `notModified` / `304` outcome exists in `core/outcomes.ts:55-67,481-482` but is never produced by a FHIR route. | `none` | — | glue — HAPI **does** implement conditional read and will return 304; either accept the superset or disable for strict parity | BB-R-014.1 |
| FE-011 | `Content-Location` header | `fhir/outcomes.ts:40-42`; `core/outcomes.ts:262-277` | `sendOutcome` sets `Content-Location` from `outcome.issue[0].diagnostics` whenever the outcome id is `accepted`. That is the *only* place it is set. ---

#### 1. Contradictions with REQUIREMENTS.md / ADRs

**BB-R-001.3** — *"`_history` per resource and per type; every version retained."*
The code registers **only** `GET :resourceType/:id/_history` (`fhir-router/fhirrouter.ts:326`). There is no type-level `GET /Patient/_history` and no system-level `GET /_history`; `getResourceInteractions` defaults to a list that includes `history-instance` but **not `history-type`** (`config/capabilitystatement.ts:10-19`). `GET /fhir/R4/Patient/_history` in Medplum falls through to the `:resourceType/:id` read route and returns **404 `not-found`**. HAPI implements type- and system-level `$history` out of the box, so Big Book will *exceed* the brick here for free — but the brick's claim that Medplum has per-type history is wrong. **Fix**: reword to "`_history` per resource instance (Medplum); type-level history is a HAPI superset Big Book keeps."

**BB-R-001.4** — *"Batch (`type: batch`) and transaction (`type: transaction`) bundles, with internal (`urn:uuid`) and conditional references, `ifMatch` version checks, PATCH entries."*
Three mismatches. (a) In Medplum a `transaction` bundle is **only** atomic when the project carries the `transaction-bundles` feature; without it `isTransaction()` is false (`fhir-router/batch.ts:873-875`) and the bundle runs as a batch, **silently**, still returning `transaction-response`. The brick reads as though transactions are unconditionally atomic. (b) PATCH entries in Medplum require a base64 `Binary` or a `Parameters` resource (`batch.ts:763-799`) — a plain JSON-Patch array is impossible in a bundle. (c) Transactions are capped at **50 updates** and, when any conditional operation is present, at **8 entries** (`batch.ts:29-30,244-254`). Neither cap is mentioned in the brick and neither exists in HAPI. **Fix**: add "transaction atomicity is unconditional in Big Book (HAPI); Medplum's `transaction-bundles` gate and its 50/8 entry caps are not reproduced" and note the PATCH entry encodings.

**BB-R-001.9** — *"Server-assigned UUID ids only; `PUT /Type/<new-id>` (update-as-create) is rejected as in Medplum (ADR-003): 404 for project users; super-admin may set ids (BB-R-005.7) and gets 200."*
Consistent with the code (`repo.ts` per the parity guide, rows d/d') and with `CapabilityStatement.updateCreate: false` (`fhir/metadata.ts:302`). **But** the brick does not cover the *conditional* update-as-create case, which behaves differently: `PUT /Patient?identifier=X` with 0 matches and a body `id` → **400** `Cannot perform create as update with client-assigned ID`, not 404 (`fhir-router/repo.ts:347-353`, parity row a2); with 0 matches and no `id` → **201** (parity row a3). Two more paths a strict-parity test must cover. **Fix**: add a sentence to 001.9.

**BB-R-001.5** — *"`$validate` against base R4 and any loaded profile."*
Medplum registers `$validate` **only at type level** (`POST /:resourceType/$validate`, `fhir/routes.ts:432-436`) and it validates the request **body**. There is no instance-level `POST /Patient/:id/$validate` and no `GET`. HAPI provides both. Superset again, but the brick should not imply instance-level `$validate` exists in Medplum.

**BB-R-003.1** — *"`POST /fhir/R4/$graphql` **and `/fhir/R4/Type/id/$graphql`**, FHIR GraphQL spec."*
**Direct contradiction.** Only `POST $graphql` is registered (`fhir-router/fhirrouter.ts:334`). There is **no** instance-level `/Type/:id/$graphql` route in Medplum at all — such a request has 3 path segments and would match `:resourceType/:id/$graph`… no, `$graphql ≠ $graph`, so it falls to `POST :resourceType` (2 segments) — segment counts differ, so it 404s. There is also no `GET /$graphql`. **Fix**: strike the instance-level path from BB-R-003.1 or mark it as a HAPI superset (HAPI does implement instance-level `$graphql`).

**BB-R-003.4** — *"Introspection togglable per project (`graphql-introspection` feature)."*
In the code introspection is a **server-wide** config key, not a project feature: `new FhirRouter({introspectionEnabled: getConfig().introspectionEnabled})` is built **once, lazily, on the first request** (`fhir/routes.ts:221-235`) and reused for the process lifetime. The three genuinely per-project GraphQL knobs are `graphqlBatchedSearchSize`, `graphqlMaxDepth` and `graphqlMaxSearches`, passed per request via `req.config` (`routes.ts:493-496`). A `graphql-introspection` project feature does not gate this route. **Fix**: correct 003.4 to "server-wide `introspectionEnabled`; depth/search-count limits are per-project system settings."

**BB-R-014.1** — *"FHIR base `/fhir/R4` with Medplum-identical paths, headers (`Content-Type: application/fhir+json`), status codes (404/410/412/422 semantics)."*
Two problems. (a) `application/fhir+json` is **not** universal: `$graphql`, `/metadata`, `$versions`, both well-knowns and the bulk manifest all return `application/json` (FE-032); `$csv` returns `text/csv`; Binary reads return the blob's own type. (b) **422 is essentially unreachable on the FHIR routes.** `getStatus` maps only `business-rule` to 422 (`core/outcomes.ts:500-501`), and `badRequest()`/`validationError()` emit **no `id`**, so schema and profile validation failures land on **400** (`core/outcomes.ts:279-293, 506-507`). The parity guide's compat-action column already says "422→400 on validation". **Fix**: change 014.1 to "404/410/412/400 semantics (422 only for `business-rule`)" and qualify the content type.

**BB-R-014.6** — *"v0.1 by HAPI config (UUID ids, no update-as-create, `_count` cap 1000, `application/fhir+json`, `ETag`); v0.2 glue: OperationOutcome `id`/`issue.code` mapping, 409→412 on `If-Match`, 422→400 on validation, `X-Trace-Id`."*
Mostly right, but the v0.2 glue list is **incomplete**. Reading the routes, these also need rewriting and none is listed: (1) **`Location` has no `/_history/<vid>` suffix** in Medplum (FE-012) while HAPI always appends it; (2) **batch entry `response.status` is a bare `"201"`** while HAPI emits `"201 Created"` (FE-021); (3) **batch entry `response.location` is relative** (`Patient/abc`) while HAPI emits absolute; (4) **DELETE returns 200 + `OperationOutcome{id:'ok'}`**, and HAPI's 200 body differs in text (parity row b); (5) **`X-Medplum: extended`** must be honoured or `@medplum/core` clients lose `meta.project`/`meta.author` (FE-015); (6) **method-not-allowed is 404, not 405** (FE-038); (7) the **`Access-Control-Expose-Headers`** list (FE-016) is load-bearing for any browser SDK; (8) `$graphql` must return `application/json` (FE-032). Also: `_count` cap 1000 is not enforced in any file in this area — it lives in `packages/server/src/fhir/search.ts` (Area 2b), so 014.6 should not imply the route layer does it. **Fix**: extend the v0.2 glue list with items 1-8.

**BB-R-002.7 / paging** — the brick says "Paging: offset (`_offset`) and cursor (`next` links)". Confirmed at the route layer only for `_history` (`_offset`/`_count` parsed at `fhir-router/fhirrouter.ts:197-198`); search paging is entirely inside the repository (Area 2b). No contradiction, just a scope note.

**ADR-003 (option B, v0.1)** — nothing in this area contradicts it. The four mount prefixes (FR-001) are an aliasing convenience; only the plain `/fhir/R4` form is needed for option B, and the `/projects/:id/` variant overlaps with BB-R-005.7's `X-Project` header decision — worth an explicit "we chose the header, not the path prefix" line in ADR-004.

---

#### 2. Notes for the Java port

##### (a) The complete path inventory under `/fhir/R4`

**Public (no authentication):**
- `GET /metadata`
- `GET /$versions`, `GET /%24versions`
- `GET /.well-known/smart-configuration`
- `GET /.well-known/smart-styles.json`

**Express-native, authenticated (bypass the FhirRouter):**
- `GET /:resourceType/$csv`, `/:resourceType/%24csv`
- `POST /$ai`, `/%24ai`
- `POST /Agent/$push`, `/Agent/:id/$push` (+ `%24` variants)
- `GET|POST /Bot/$execute`, `/Bot/:id/$execute`, `/Bot/$execute/*splat`, `/Bot/:id/$execute/*splat` (+ `%24` variants) — 8 paths × 2 methods
- `GET|DELETE /bulkdata/export/:id`
- `GET|DELETE /job/:id/status`
- `POST|PUT|GET /Binary`, `/Binary/:id` — mounted at `app.ts:213`, **before the body parsers**

**FhirRouter core REST (`fhir-router/fhirrouter.ts:320-334`), 15 routes:**

| Method | Path | Interaction |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `` (empty) | search-system (`_type` required) |
| POST | `` (empty) | batch / transaction |
| GET | `:resourceType` | search-type |
| POST | `:resourceType/_search` | search-type |
| POST | `:resourceType` | create (+ `If-None-Exist` → conditional create) |
| GET | `:resourceType/:id` | read |
| GET | `:resourceType/:id/_history` | history-instance |
| GET | `:resourceType/:id/_history/:vid` | vread |
| PUT | `:resourceType/:id` | update |
| PUT | `:resourceType` | conditional update / upsert |
| DELETE | `:resourceType/:id` | delete |
| DELETE | `:resourceType` | conditional delete |
| PATCH | `:resourceType/:id` | patch |
| PATCH | `:resourceType` | conditional patch |
| POST | `$graphql` | operation |

**Not present:** `GET /_history`, `GET /:resourceType/_history`, `GET /$graphql`, `GET|POST /:resourceType/:id/$graphql`, `GET /:resourceType/:id/$validate`, `HEAD` on anything, and any generic `/:resourceType/$:operation` fallthrough. Percent-encoded `%24` is registered only on the Express-native routes, never on FhirRouter operations.

**`$export` at three levels** (all registered on the FhirRouter, `fhir/routes.ts:242-243,350-355`):
- **System**: `GET|POST /$export` → `bulkExportHandler`
- **Patient**: `GET|POST /Patient/$export` → `patientExportHandler`
- **Group**: `GET|POST /Group/:id/$export` → `groupExportHandler`

All three ignore `Prefer: respond-async` and are **unconditionally asynchronous**: they create a `BulkDataExport`, kick off the work in a detached promise, and return `[accepted(<baseUrl>/fhir/R4/bulkdata/export/<id>)]` → **202** + `Content-Location`. They read `_since` and `_type` (comma-separated) from the query; `_outputFormat`, `_typeFilter` and `_elements` are ignored.

**Operation dispatch** (`fhir/routes.ts:233-467`, ~90 `router.add` calls): type-level and instance-level operations are **individually registered constants**. Matching (`fhir-router/urlrouter.ts:72-86`) requires an exact segment-count match and picks the candidate with the most literal segments, so `/Patient/:id/$everything` (3 literal-ish segments) beats `/:resourceType/:id/$graph` only because the operation names differ — there is no ambiguity in practice. Four operations are registered generically across all types: `$graph` (GET), `$set-accounts`, `$refresh-reference-display`, `$expunge`, `$reindex`, `$resend`, `$aws-textract` (all POST at instance level) and `$validate` (POST at type level). Anything unmatched falls to `tryCustomOperation` (Bot-backed `OperationDefinition`) before 404.

**Conditional operations** (all four verbs): conditional create via `If-None-Exist` header (POST only), conditional update/delete/patch via a query string on the type-level path. All four run under a **serializable** repository transaction with `count=2` and dropped sort rules.

**SDK coverage of the path inventory.** Eleven server routes have **no** `MedplumClient` method at all: `/metadata`, `/$versions`, `/.well-known/smart-configuration`, `POST /:resourceType/_search`, conditional `DELETE`, conditional `PATCH`, `GET` and `DELETE /job/:id/status`, `GET` and `DELETE /bulkdata/export/:id`, and `$expunge`. Two more (`$csv`, `$resend`) are used by the Medplum **app** only via hand-built `medplum.fhirUrl(...)` strings (`app/src/HomePage.tsx:66`, `app/src/resource/ResendSubscriptionsModal.tsx:31`). This matters for BB-R-014.5's "v0.2 = `@medplum/core` runs unmodified" target: **an SDK-grade conformance harness only exercises about two-thirds of the surface below**, and the untested third (`_search` POST, conditional DELETE/PATCH, the bulk manifest) is exactly where HAPI diverges most. The bulk manifest at `/bulkdata/export/:id` is reached only implicitly, by `pollStatus()` following the `Content-Location` we hand out — which means a Big Book that emits HAPI's own `$export-poll-status` URL in that header would work with `@medplum/core` unchanged, and the Medplum-shaped alias is optional. Flag for the v0.2 scope call.

##### (b) The `OperationOutcome.id` slug vocabulary

Defined as module constants in `packages/core/src/outcomes.ts:8-25`, consumed by `getStatus` (`:471-509`) and by the `isX` predicates that `@medplum/core` clients switch on (`:437-469`). Complete list, constant name → slug → HTTP status → `issue[0].code`:

| Constant | Slug | Status | `issue.code` | Producer |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `OK_ID` | `ok` | 200 | `informational` | `allOk`, `redirectOk` |
| `CREATED_ID` | `created` | 201 | `informational` | `created` |
| `ACCEPTED_ID` | `accepted` | 202 | `informational` | `accepted(location)` — `diagnostics` = the URL |
| `FOUND_ID` | `found` | 302 | `informational` | `redirect(url)` — URL in `details.coding[system='urn:ietf:rfc:3986'].code` |
| `NOT_MODIFIED_ID` | `not-modified` | 304 | `informational` | `notModified` (never produced by a FHIR route) |
| `UNAUTHORIZED_ID` | `unauthorized` | 401 | `login` | `unauthorized`, `unauthorizedTokenExpired` (+`expired`), `unauthorizedTokenAudience` (+`invalid`) |
| `FORBIDDEN_ID` | `forbidden` | 403 | `forbidden` | `forbidden` |
| `NOT_FOUND_ID` | `not-found` | 404 | `not-found` | `notFound` |
| `CONFLICT_ID` | `conflict` | 409 | `conflict` | `conflict(details, code?)` — optional `details.coding[0].code` |
| `GONE_ID` | `gone` | 410 | `deleted` | `gone` |
| `PRECONDITION_FAILED_ID` | `precondition-failed` | 412 | `processing` | `preconditionFailed` |
| `MULTIPLE_MATCHES_ID` | `multiple-matches` | 412 | `multiple-matches` | `multipleMatches` |
| `CONTENT_TOO_LARGE_ID` | `content-too-large` | 413 | `too-long` | `contentTooLarge(text)` |
| `UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA_TYPE_ID` | `unsupported-media-type` | 415 | `not-supported` | `unsupportedMediaType` |
| `BUSINESS_RULE` | `business-rule` | 422 | `business-rule` | `businessRule(key, message)` — `details.id` = the key |
| `TOO_MANY_REQUESTS_ID` | `too-many-requests` | 429 | `throttled` | `tooManyRequests` + `rate-limit-reset` extension |
| `SERVER_TIMEOUT_ID` | `server-timeout` | 504 | `timeout` | `serverTimeout(msg?)` |

**Three factories emit no `id` at all**, and that is load-bearing:
- `badRequest(details, expression?)` → `issue.code: 'invalid'` → falls to the default branch → **400**
- `validationError(details, expressions?, code?, diagnostics?)` → `issue.code: code ?? 'structure'` → **400**
- `serverError(err)` → `issue.code: 'exception'` → the default branch's exception check → **500**

The default branch is `outcome.issue?.[0]?.code === 'exception' ? 500 : 400` (`:506-508`). So **any** OperationOutcome Big Book invents without a recognised `id` will be read by `@medplum/core` as a plain bad request. The ten slugs the parity guide names as SDK-critical (`ok`, `created`, `not-found`, `gone`, `precondition-failed`, `multiple-matches`, `conflict`, `unauthorized`, `forbidden`, `bad-request`) are a subset of this table — note `bad-request` is **not** a real slug; it is the *absence* of an id.

`RATE_LIMIT_RESET_EXTENSION_URL = 'https://medplum.com/fhir/StructureDefinition/rate-limit-reset'` (`:22`) carries a `valueUnsignedInt` in **seconds**, and `getRateLimitReset` falls back to parsing `issue.diagnostics` as JSON looking for `msBeforeNext`.

##### (c) Batch vs transaction in Medplum

1. **The gate.** `isTransaction()` = `bundle.type === 'transaction' && req.config.transactions`, and `req.config.transactions = project.features.includes('transaction-bundles')` (`fhir/routes.ts:498`). **Without the feature a transaction bundle degrades to batch semantics silently** — no error, no warning, and the response is still typed `transaction-response`. Big Book should make transactions unconditionally atomic (HAPI does) and *not* reproduce the gate; but any Medplum migration script that assumed the gate was off will behave differently.

2. **Ordering.** Entries are **reordered before execution**, bucketed by REST interaction into the fixed sequence `transaction → batch → delete → create → update → patch → operation → search-system → search-type → read → vread → history-*` (`fhir-router/batch.ts:398-414,474-477`). Client entry order is not preserved. HAPI's `TransactionProcessor` orders DELETE → POST → PUT/PATCH → GET, which is close but not identical (Medplum runs *nested bundles* first and *reads last*).

3. **`urn:uuid` resolution.** Ids are assigned **eagerly at preprocess time** by `repo.generateId()` and stored in a `resolvedIdentities` map keyed by the entry's `fullUrl` (`batch.ts:554-569`). Only **then** are entries executed, and each entry is deep-walked to replace any string matching `urn(:|%3A)uuid(:|%3A)<uuid>` (`:32`, `:862-871`). Consequences: (i) forward *and* backward references both work — a Patient may reference an Observation declared later; (ii) the match is a **substring** replace, so `"urn:uuid:abc"` embedded inside a longer string (a FHIRPath expression, a narrative) is also rewritten; (iii) percent-encoded URNs inside batch entry URLs are handled; (iv) on a serialization retry, preprocessing is deliberately **not** re-run so ids stay stable (`:198-217`).

4. **Conditional references.** A conditional URL (`PUT /Patient?identifier=X`, `DELETE /Patient?…`, `PATCH /Patient?…`) is resolved by a `count=2` search **at preprocess time** and `entry.request.url` is **rewritten to the absolute `Type/id`** (`:615`), so the entry executes unconditionally. 0 matches: DELETE is skipped, PUT with a body `id` → `Cannot provide ID for create by update`, PUT without id gets a fresh id, PATCH → `Conditional PATCH did not match any resources`. 2 matches → `Conditional <M> matched multiple resources`. `ifNoneExist` on a create resolves to the **existing** resource's reference if exactly one match, so downstream `urn:uuid` references point at the pre-existing resource. **Note the resolution errors are `badRequest` (400), not `multipleMatches` (412)** — a conditional PUT that would return 412 standalone returns a 400-shaped entry inside a bundle.

5. **Per-entry errors.** Batch: the error becomes the entry's `response.outcome` with `response.status` set from `getStatus`, and processing continues. **A 429 short-circuits**: every remaining entry (in *ordering* order) is filled with the rate-limit outcome (`:683-690`). Transaction: any non-ok entry outcome throws out and the whole bundle returns a top-level 400.

6. **Entry response shape.** `{response: {outcome, status: '<decimal>', location: 'Type/id' | undefined}, resource}` (`:892-901`). `status` is a bare number string (`"201"`), **not** HAPI's `"201 Created"`; `location` is relative, **not** HAPI's absolute URL; `outcome` is always present, which HAPI only does on failure.

7. **Limits.** Transaction only: >50 update entries → 400; any conditional operation promotes to SERIALIZABLE and then >8 entries → 400; duplicate resolved identity → 400.

8. **`async-batch`.** A **separate** project feature from `transaction-bundles`. `POST /fhir/R4` with `Prefer: respond-async` requires `async-batch`, parses with `maxBatchSize` (50 MB) instead of `maxJsonSize` (1 MB), rejects `transaction` bundles **when `transaction-bundles` is also on**, and returns 202 + `Content-Location` to `/fhir/R4/job/:id/status`. Work in the job is exempt from the caller's FHIR quota. The re-entrant processor (`BatchProcessor.preprocess`/`fromState`/`processNextEntry`, `batch.ts:167-181,285-316`) checkpoints `{mutated bundle, bundleInfo, resolvedIdentities, preprocessResults}` plus a position marker so a worker crash resumes without re-assigning ids — a design note worth copying if Big Book ever does async batch.

##### (d) `Prefer: respond-async` at the FHIR routes level

Exactly **five** handlers read it, and only one is in `/fhir/R4`'s generic path:

| Route | Behaviour | Source |
|---|---|---|
| `POST /fhir/R4` (bundle) | Optional. Requires the `async-batch` project feature (400 otherwise). 202 + `Content-Location`. Rejects `transaction` when `transaction-bundles` is on. | `async-batch.ts:16-55` |
| `GET\|POST /fhir/R4/Bot[/:id]/$execute` | Optional. Wraps the bot run in `sendAsyncResponse`. | `fhir/operations/execute.ts:38` |
| `POST /fhir/R4/Agent[/:id]/$push` | Optional. | `fhir/operations/agentpush.ts:34` |
| `POST /:resourceType/:id/$set-accounts` | Optional, **and only when the `propagate` parameter is set**. | `fhir/operations/set-accounts.ts:93` |
| `POST /$db-configure-indexes` | **Mandatory** — 400 `Operation requires "Prefer: respond-async"` without it. | `fhir/operations/db-configure-indexes.ts:56` |

(`admin/super.ts:743` is the same mandatory pattern outside `/fhir/R4`.)

Everything else ignores the header: plain create/read/update/delete/patch/search, `$graphql`, `$validate`, `$everything`, `$expunge`. **`$export` is the important asymmetry** — it never checks `Prefer` and is *always* async, which is non-conformant to the Bulk Data IG (which requires `Prefer: respond-async`) but is what Medplum does. Matching is `req.get('prefer') !== 'respond-async'` — an **exact string compare**, so `Prefer: respond-async, return=minimal` or `Prefer: respond-async;handling=strict` is **not** recognised. A Spring implementation should parse the header properly (superset) but must accept the bare form.

##### (e) What HAPI JPA does differently — the BB-R-014.1/14.6 glue list

Ordered by how likely it is to break an `@medplum/core` client:

1. **`OperationOutcome.id` is absent in HAPI.** HAPI's `OperationOutcome` has no `id` and its `issue.code` vocabulary differs (`processing` where Medplum uses `deleted`/`multiple-matches`). `@medplum/core`'s `isOk`/`isNotFound`/`isGone`/`isConflict`/`isUnauthenticated` switch on `id`, **not** on HTTP status. Without a translating `@ControllerAdvice` every SDK error branch misfires. **This is the single highest-value piece of glue in the whole area.** The full slug table is §(b).
2. **Version conflict: HAPI 409, Medplum 412.** `ResourceVersionConflictException` → 409; Medplum returns 412 `id=precondition-failed`, `issue.code=processing` (parity row e). Also the two 412 flavours must be distinguished: `precondition-failed` (stale `If-Match`) vs `multiple-matches` (conditional operation matched >1).
3. **Update-as-create.** HAPI returns 201 + `Location` for `PUT /Patient/<fresh-id>`; Medplum returns **404 `not-found`** for project users and **200 with `ETag` and no `Location`** for super-admin (parity rows d/d'). Requires `client_id_strategy=NONE` plus an interceptor that maps the resulting rejection to 404 (and a super-admin bypass per BB-R-005.7). `CapabilityStatement.updateCreate` must be forced to `false`.
4. **`Location` suffix.** HAPI: `Location: https://…/fhir/R4/Patient/abc/_history/1`. Medplum: `https://…/fhir/R4/Patient/abc` (no history suffix, `fhir/response.ts:19-21,30-32`). Strip it.
5. **Batch entry response shape.** HAPI: `response.status = "201 Created"` (status + reason phrase), `response.location` absolute, `response.outcome` present only on failure. Medplum: `"201"`, relative `Patient/abc`, `outcome` always present. Rewrite in a bundle post-processor.
6. **`urn:uuid` placeholder resolution.** HAPI resolves placeholders during transaction execution in its own dependency order; Medplum assigns ids in a *preprocess pass* and then does a **string-level** substring replace over the entire entry (`batch.ts:862-871`). Differences that bite: Medplum rewrites URNs inside narrative text, extension values and FHIRPath strings; HAPI only rewrites `Reference.reference` and canonical fields. Also, Medplum's bucketed reordering (delete → create → update → patch → operation → search → read) differs from HAPI's. A bundle that works in Medplum may fail in HAPI if it relied on a read entry seeing a write from the same bundle.
7. **Conditional operations in bundles return 400, not 412.** Medplum's preprocess-time resolution errors are `badRequest` (`Conditional PUT matched multiple resources`), whereas the standalone route returns 412 `multiple-matches`. HAPI is consistent (412 both ways). If a Medplum client special-cases the 400, it will need the HAPI behaviour instead — recommend **not** reproducing Medplum's inconsistency, but documenting it.
8. **DELETE body.** Both return 200, but Medplum's body is `OperationOutcome{id:'ok', issue:[{code:'informational', text:'All OK'}]}` with **no `ETag`/`Location`**; HAPI's says "…was already deleted" on the second delete (parity row b). Rewrite the body.
9. **Deleted resource read.** Medplum: 410 `id=gone`, `issue.code=deleted`, text "Gone". HAPI: 410, `issue.code=processing`, "Resource was deleted at …" (parity row c). Rewrite both fields.
10. **Method-not-allowed.** Medplum's router matches on exact segment count, so a wrong verb on a valid path yields **404**; HAPI returns **405**. A strict-parity harness will catch this. Cheap interceptor.
11. **`_format` and XML.** HAPI honours `_format=xml` and serves XML by default. Medplum has no `_format` at all and advertises `format: ['json']` only. Narrow the `FhirContext` / restrict `Accept` handling, or accept the superset and document it.
12. **Conditional read.** HAPI implements `If-None-Match`/`If-Modified-Since` and will return **304**; Medplum never returns 304 and advertises `conditionalRead: 'not-supported'`. Superset — safe to leave on, but the CapabilityStatement must say `not-supported` if strict parity matters.
13. **Type- and system-level `_history`.** HAPI has them; Medplum does not (see §1). Superset.
14. **Instance-level `$graphql`.** HAPI has it; Medplum does not. Superset.
15. **`X-Medplum: extended`.** Nothing in HAPI. Big Book must implement it: without the header, strip the six `meta` fields (`author`, `project`, `account`, `accounts`, `compartment`, `deleted`) from every read; with it (and super/project-admin) allow writing `meta.account`. `MedplumClient` always sends it, so an SDK-grade client will *expect* the extended fields.
16. **JSON serialisation.** Medplum's `stringify` recursively strips `null`, `undefined`, `""`, `{}` and `[]`. HAPI's parser omits nulls but **keeps empty arrays**, and JSON responses differ byte-for-byte. Configure Jackson `NON_EMPTY` plus an empty-collection filter if byte parity is ever tested.
17. **Content types per endpoint.** `$graphql`, `/metadata`, `$versions`, the well-knowns and the bulk manifest are `application/json` in Medplum (FE-032). HAPI serves `/metadata` and `$graphql` as `application/fhir+json`. Cheap `produces=` overrides.
18. **`Access-Control-Expose-Headers`.** HAPI's `CorsInterceptor` defaults do not include `Content-Location`, `ETag`, `Last-Modified`, `Location`, `RateLimit`, `X-Request-Id`, `X-Trace-Id`. Without them a browser SDK cannot read the ETag or the async poll URL. **Mandatory glue for BB-R-014.2/13.**
19. **Batch PATCH entry encoding.** HAPI accepts a `Parameters` FHIRPath Patch in a bundle entry; it does **not** accept Medplum's base64-`Binary`-containing-a-JSON-Patch-array convention (`batch.ts:766-772`). Either add a bundle pre-processor or document the incompatibility.
20. **Async poll URL.** Medplum: `/fhir/R4/job/:id/status` (AsyncJob body, 200/202) and `/fhir/R4/bulkdata/export/:id` (manifest, 200/202-empty/404-on-cancelled). HAPI: `$export-poll-status?_jobId=…` returning a `Parameters`/manifest. Any Medplum client polling loop breaks; BB-R-020's glue must expose the Medplum-shaped URLs (the `Content-Location` we hand out is what the client follows, so a thin alias is enough).
21. **Body size.** `maxJsonSize` defaults to **1 MB** in Medplum, and a 1.1 MB create is rejected with **413 `id=content-too-large`, `issue.code=too-long`**. HAPI/Spring defaults are larger. Set the limit explicitly and map the Spring `MaxUploadSizeExceededException` to that outcome.
22. **Presigned attachment rewriting is a response-body transform** (FE-030), not an endpoint. Every authenticated FHIR response walks the body and replaces `url`/`path` strings pointing at a Binary with a presigned URL, **reading the Binary through the caller's own access policy** so unreadable attachments degrade to `Binary/<id>` rather than leaking. HAPI has no equivalent; this is a Big Book interceptor when BB-R-009.3 lands in v0.2.
23. **The tracing extension.** Every Medplum error body carries `extension[url=https://medplum.com/fhir/StructureDefinition/tracing]` with `requestId`/`traceId` sub-extensions. The parity guide already flags adding it as a v0.2 decision; note that Medplum puts it on **error bodies only**, not on success bodies.
24. **Query-string cap.** 70,000 characters → 400 (`urlrouter.ts:13`). Mainly relevant to batch entry URLs. One guard.
25. **Binary write path bypasses body parsing.** Medplum mounts the Binary router *above* all parsers so gigabyte uploads stream to storage (`app.ts:213`, comment `binary.ts:95-99`), and handles `Content-Encoding: gzip|deflate` itself. In Spring this means a dedicated `HttpServletRequest.getInputStream()` controller outside the normal `@RequestBody` path, or HAPI's `BinaryStorageInterceptor`.

---

### 2.2 Repository — CRUD, versioning, storage, multi-tenancy


Source snapshot: medplum `packages/server/src/fhir` (`repo.ts`, `repository/*`, `sharding.ts`, `precommit.ts`, `resource-cap.ts`, `fhirquota.ts`, `patient.ts`, `references.ts`, `structure.ts`, `accesspolicy.ts`, `searchparameter.ts`, `lookups/*`) plus `packages/fhir-router/src/repo.ts` and `fhirrouter.ts`, at `main` `fbc8e7b4b`. `Source` paths are relative to `packages/server/src` unless prefixed; `fhir-router/…` and `core/…` are `packages/fhir-router/src/…` and `packages/core/src/…`. BB-R mapping against REQUIREMENTS.md at big-book `4a94f31`.

Conventions used in Behaviour: "system repo" = `SystemRepository` (`superAdmin: true`, `strictMode: true`, `extendedMode: true`, `author: {reference: 'system'}`, **no `projects`** — `repo.ts:2789-2810`); "user repo" = `getRepoForLogin` output (`accesspolicy.ts:74-88`: `projects` = own project + linked + synthetic R4, `superAdmin` = `project.superAdmin`, `projectAdmin` = `membership.admin`, `strictMode`/`checkReferencesOnWrite`/`validateTerminology` from the `Project`); "admin repo" = a user repo with `projectAdmin: true`. There is no separate admin `Repository` class — `SystemRepository extends Repository` adds nothing (`repo.ts:2777`); every difference is a context flag. Status codes already observed are cited from `docs/guides/medplum-parity.md` rather than re-derived.

| ID | Capability | Source (file:line) | Behaviour | Doc ref | Used by | Proposed fate | BB-R |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| REPO-001 | `Repository` shape: one per HTTP request | `repo.ts:329-409`, `context.ts` | `Repository extends FhirRepository implements Disposable`.
