## Area 4 — configuration (draft)

### 4.1 Server config keys, Project settings/secrets, feature flags


Source snapshot: medplum `main` @ `fbc8e7b4b` (2026-09-11, server 5.1.x); big-book @ `45dc7b0` (ADR-006 decided). `Source` paths are relative to `packages/server/src` unless stated; `Project.d.ts` = `packages/fhirtypes/dist/Project.d.ts`. Docs snapshot `docs/medplum-docs/self-hosting/{server-config,setting-configuration,project-settings}.md` scraped 2026-09-12.

Disposition vocabulary (per the area brief): `keep` (Big Book needs an equivalent — proposed Spring property given), `→ HAPI` (HAPI/Spring already has it — property named), `→ Keycloak` (realm/client config), `wire Keycloak` (Table B only), `drop` (Medplum- or AWS-specific / non-goal, with reason), `defer v0.x`.

Every key in Table A is declared in `config/types.ts` (`MedplumServerConfig` and its nested interfaces). Defaults come from `config/utils.ts:17-84` (`addDefaults`) unless the "Default" cell says otherwise; a bare `—` means no default is applied anywhere and the value is `undefined` at runtime. "Required?" means *required for the server to start or for the named feature to work*, not "declared non-optional in TypeScript" — the only key whose absence throws at load time is `baseUrl` (`config/loader.ts:63-65`).

---

##### Area 4 — decisions needed

**D** = design call (changes a brick or an ADR line), **T** = text fix. One "bless" applies all recommendations, or answer by number.

| # | Topic | What Medplum does | What the docs say now | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D32 | Config *sources* (BB-R-011.3 "env/values only") | Five loaders (`file:`, `env`, `aws:`, `gcp:`, `azure:`), selected by a **single CLI argument** (`index.ts:61`), comma-separated and deep-merged left-to-right (`loader.ts:48-71`). There is no automatic env-overrides-file precedence: `env` only participates if the operator writes it into the arg. No Docker-secrets loader exists. | BB-R-011.3 "Configuration by env/values only"; ARCHITECTURE.md §5 "Environment variables → Helm `values.yaml` / compose `.env` → defaults in `application.yml`" | BB-R-011.3 is **achievable and simpler** than Medplum: Spring Boot's `PropertySource` order gives env-over-file for free, no loader code. Adopt Spring's native order; declare AWS SSM / GCP SM / Azure KV loaders **drop** for v0.x (Vault via Spring Cloud Vault in `full` only, per BIGBOOK stack table). See Note (a). |
| D33 | Redis is a hard dependency of Medplum's server (5 optional splits: `redis`, `cacheRedis`, `rateLimitRedis`, `pubSubRedis`, `backgroundJobsRedis`) | `initRedis` is unconditional (`redis.ts:44-47`); rate limiting, BullMQ queues, websockets, FHIRcast, server registry and the resource cache all require it. | ADR-006 / BB-R-011.1: `lite` = three containers (Postgres, Keycloak, Big Book). No Redis. | Confirm **no Redis in `lite`**: rate limits are BB-R-023/v0.2 and wired to Traefik, not Redis; subscriptions are HAPI's own (DB-backed queue); no websockets in v0.1 (BB-R-007.8 deferred). So every `redis.*` key is `drop` in v0.1. If `full` later needs a shared cache, that is a new ADR, not a config key. |
| D34 | `defaultSuperAdminEmail` / `defaultSuperAdminPassword` defaults | Fall back to `admin@example.com` / `medplum_admin` **silently**, with no warning and no forced change (`seed.ts:63-64`). | BB-R-005.7: "Bootstrapped from env at first start (`BIGBOOK_ADMIN_EMAIL`/`_PASSWORD`)" | Keep the env bootstrap but make it **fail-closed**: no default password; if `bigbook.bootstrap.admin-password` is unset, generate a random one and print it once to stdout (the `lite` 10-minute install needs a value, not a guessable one). See Note (e) item S1. |
| D35 | `Project.setting` / `secret` are stored **plaintext** in the `Project` resource row | `Project.secret` is readable by any project admin through a plain FHIR read of `Project` (area 1 D5 established the same for `ClientApplication.secret`); `systemSecret` is hidden from project admins only by the injected admin policy (area 1 T1), not by encryption. | BB-R-011.3 "secrets never in values files"; BB-R-005.6 "secrets in Keycloak client attrs or env for `lite`; Vault in `full`" | BB-R-011.3 governs *values files*, which is a weaker promise than *at rest*. State explicitly in BB-R-005.6 that `Project.secret` in `lite` is **plaintext at rest in Postgres, admin-readable**, exactly like Medplum — and that Vault (`full`) is the only configuration where it is not. Otherwise the brick over-promises. See Note (c). |
| D36 | `rateLimitsEnabled` default is `true` but the shipped dev config sets it `false`; the `defaultRateLimit` sentinel `-1` disables the limiter wholesale | `config/utils.ts:51-53`; `ratelimit.ts:65`; `medplum.config.json` ships `"rateLimitsEnabled": false` | BB-R-023 "Rate limits per project/user — wire Traefik — v0.2" | Keep the split: Traefik handles HTTP rate limits (BB-R-023, v0.2), and **there is no v0.1 limiter at all**. Add one line to BB-R-023 noting Medplum has three categories (login/auth/default) and a separate FHIR *quota* (cost-weighted per interaction) — Traefik covers the first, the quota is a separate v0.2+ decision. See Contradictions §BB-R-023. |
| D37 | `allowUnsafeOutbound` | Off by default; when on, **all** SSRF protections are dropped at once (`util/url.ts:110,129-130`) — private IPs, plain HTTP and unsafe hostnames together. Subscriptions, bots and auto-download all fetch through it. | Not mentioned in any brick | Big Book needs this for `lite`: a rest-hook to `http://localhost:5678` (n8n, BB-R-008) or to a mail catcher is exactly a "private/local network" target. Recommend `bigbook.outbound.allowed-private-cidrs` (an **allow-list**, not a boolean) so `lite` can reach the compose network without disabling SSRF protection globally. ~40 glue lines. Add to BB-R-007. |
| D38 | `capabilityStatement` customisation surface (overlay, include/exclude resource types, per-type interactions, `supportedProfiles`) | 5 sub-keys, `fhir/metadata.ts:222-290` | BB-R-014.1 "Medplum-identical paths, headers, status codes" — nothing about `/metadata` | HAPI has none of this as config; it generates a CapabilityStatement from the registered providers. **defer v0.2**; if Medplum-parity smoke tests compare `/metadata`, that is a v1.0 app-grade concern (ADR-003). Note as a GAP in BB-R-014. |
| D39 | `enabledSearchParameters` (allow-list of `SearchParameter.id`) | `fhir/structure.ts:17` — default is all enabled; Medplum's own params are force-enabled regardless | Not mentioned | HAPI's equivalent is registering/removing `SearchParameter` resources (they are data, not config). Recommend **drop as a config key**, document the HAPI data-level equivalent in `docs/guides/config.md`. |
| D40 | `preCommitSubscriptionsEnabled` — a *server* key AND a *project setting*, ANDed | `fhir/precommit.ts:65-66`: fires only when server flag is on **and** project setting is on | Not in any brick | Medplum's "interceptor pattern" (synchronous pre-commit hook) overlaps ADR-001's `STORAGE_PRESTORAGE_*` hooks, which Big Book already has. Declare **drop** as config; the capability exists natively. Note in ADR-001 as a resolved overlap. |
| D41 | `workers.enabled` + the two partial config files (`medplum-workers-partial.config.json`, `medplum-no-workers-partial.config.json`) | The shipped pattern for splitting an HTTP-only pool from a worker pool, composed via the comma-separated config arg. | ADR-006: `lite` = one Big Book JVM. | `lite` runs everything in one JVM; **drop** for v0.1. Worth one sentence in `docs/guides/config.md` as the documented scale-out story for `full` (Spring profiles, not a config key) — matches BB-R-011.2. |

##### Text fixes

| # | Doc | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| T37 | BB-R-005.6 settings list | `rateLimit` is `Project.systemSetting` (super-admin only), not `Project.setting`, and it is one of **three** rate-limit system settings (`rateLimit`, `authRateLimit`, `loginRateLimit`) plus four quota ones (`userFhirQuota`, `totalFhirQuota`, `enableFhirQuota`, `resourceCap`). Already flagged as area 1 T9; this area confirms with read sites. |
| T38 | Medplum's own `setting-configuration.md` | Says "When both a configuration file and environment variables are present, the environment variables will take precedence." **False.** Precedence is purely the left-to-right order of the comma-separated CLI arg (`loader.ts:54-61`); `file:x.json` alone never reads `MEDPLUM_*`. Do not copy this sentence into Big Book docs. |
| T39 | Medplum's `server-config.md` | Documents `auditEventLogGroup` and `auditEventLogStream` (lines 275-286). Neither exists in `config/types.ts` nor is read anywhere in `packages/server/src`. Stale doc; ignore. |
| T40 | BB-R-011.3 "every key documented in one table" | Medplum's own `server-config.md` documents ~90 of the ~150 flattened keys (Table A below). The gap is mostly nested database/redis/bullmq tuning keys. Big Book's table must be generated from the `@ConfigurationProperties` classes, not hand-written, or it will drift the same way. Recommend a test that fails when a property has no doc row. |
| T41 | BB-R-004.3 `mfaRequired` | Correct that it is a project setting, but incomplete: the effective value is `Project.setting[mfaRequired].valueBoolean OR User.mfaRequired` (`auth/utils.ts:129`), the project value **wins over** a user `false`, and there is a sibling setting `allowedMfaMethods` (`totp`/`email`, `auth/utils.ts:55`) plus `appName` for white-labelling. Area 1 D7 already raised email-OTP; this adds `appName`. |

#### Table B — Project settings / secrets / systemSettings

**Structural facts established by the read-site sweep:**

- There is **no generic accessor** — no `getProjectSetting`, `getSystemSetting`, `getProjectSecret` anywhere in `packages/server/src` or `packages/core/src`. Every read is an inline `find()` against one named field. Consequently **`systemSetting` never falls back to `setting`** (and vice versa): each name lives in exactly one field, fixed by its call site. Only domain-specific wrappers exist (`getProjectSmtpConfig`, `isMfaRequired`, `getUserFhirQuota`, …).
- `Project.systemSecret` is **never read by name** — it is only merged wholesale into the bot secrets map (PS-033).
- `config.defaultProjectSystemSetting` (CFG-125) is a **seed applied at project creation only** (`fhir/operations/projectinit.ts:174`), not a runtime fallback. There is no `defaultProjectSetting`/`defaultProjectSecret`/`defaultProjectSystemSecret`.
- Access-policy exposure (`fhir/accesspolicy.ts:341-348`): to a **project admin**, `systemSetting` is readonly and `systemSecret`/`superAdmin`/`strictMode` are hidden — admins read/write `setting` + `secret` only. To a **non-admin member**, all four fields are hidden.
- `ProjectSetting` value slots are `valueString` | `valueBoolean` | `valueInteger` | `valueDecimal` (`Project.d.ts:252-278`); no `valueDecimal` name is used by any read site.

| ID | Name | Where | Type | Default | Effect | Source (file:line) | Big Book disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PS-001 | `mfaRequired` | `setting` | valueBoolean | falsy | ORed with `User.mfaRequired` — a project can only **tighten**, never relax, a user-level requirement. Existing and new users must enroll at their next password login. | `auth/utils.ts:129` | `wire Keycloak` — conditional-OTP flow per organisation (BB-R-004.3; area 1 D38 sets it v0.2) |
| PS-002 | `allowedMfaMethods` | `setting` | valueString (comma-delimited `totp`,`email`) | `['totp']` when unset, blank, or no valid token survives filtering | Which MFA factors a user may enroll in. | `auth/utils.ts:55` | `wire Keycloak` (`totp` only) — email OTP is **non-goal v0.x** (area 1 D38; Keycloak has no native email OTP) |
| PS-003 | `appName` | `setting` | valueString (trimmed; blank → `undefined`) | `undefined` (everything names "Medplum") | White-labels user-facing content: email sender display name, MFA email body, TOTP authenticator issuer. | `email/utils.ts:114` | `wire Keycloak` — realm display name + email theme (BB-R-010.2, defer v0.2 for branded templates) |

#### Table C — feature flags

The `project-feature` CodeSystem/ValueSet lives in `packages/definitions/src/fhir/r4/valuesets-medplum.json` (`url: https://medplum.com/fhir/CodeSystem/project-feature`, `content: complete`; the ValueSet composes the whole CodeSystem). The TypeScript union at `packages/fhirtypes/dist/Project.d.ts:141-143` mirrors all 17 codes in the same order — no drift.

There is **no centralised feature helper**. Almost every site is an inline `project.features?.includes('x')`. The three exceptions are local: `hasProjectFeature` (module-private, one call site, `ws/ai-realtime.ts:223`), `isBotEnabledForProject` (`bots/utils.ts:157`, bots only, does its own `systemRepo` read), and `Repository.supportsRangeSearch()` (`fhir/repo.ts:2034`).

`features` is echoed to clients in `/auth/me` (`auth/me.ts:96`) and is `readonly` for project admins in the injected admin policy (`fhir/accesspolicy.ts:341`), so a project cannot self-grant a flag. Stored as `Project.features TEXT[]` (`migrations/schema/v107.ts:14`) with a GIN index (`migrations/data/v37.ts:22`).

| ID | Feature | Read sites (file:line) | What it gates | Dead flag? | Big Book disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FF-001 | `ai` | `fhir/operations/ai.ts:130` | The whole `$ai` operation — returns `forbidden` before it even looks for the `OPENAI_API_KEY` secret (PS-031). (UI-only extra: `examples/medplum-provider/src/pages/spaces/SpacesPage.tsx:24` requires `bots` AND `ai`.) | no (1 server site) | `drop` — AI features are an explicit non-goal |
| FF-002 | `ai-realtime` | `ws/ai-realtime.ts:144` (via module-private `hasProjectFeature`, `:223`) | The `/ws/ai-realtime` OpenAI realtime-transcription proxy: sends an error frame and closes with 1008 before opening the upstream socket. | no (1 server site) | `drop` (with FF-001) |

| FF-012 | `websocket-subscriptions` | `fhir/operations/getwsbindingtoken.ts:146`; `workers/subscription.ts:566` | `$get-ws-binding-token` returns `badRequest('WebSocket subscriptions not enabled for current project')`, so no socket can be opened. In the matching worker, only with the flag on does the WS-subscription cache-miss detection / GC branch run (logs `'Excessive subscription cache miss'`, prunes inactive subs at ≥5 misses). (Client: `packages/react-hooks/src/useSubscription/useSubscription.ts:54` no-ops without it.) | no (2 sites) | `defer v0.2` — BB-R-007.8 |
| FF-013 | `transaction-bundles` | `fhir/routes.ts:498`; `async-batch.ts:36` | `routes.ts:498` sets `FhirRequest.config.transactions`, consumed at `fhir-router/src/batch.ts:874` (`isTransaction()`): **without the flag a `transaction` Bundle degrades to batch semantics** — no DB transaction, no atomicity, no serialization-failure retry (`batch.ts:199`). `async-batch.ts:36` rejects a `transaction` Bundle submitted with `Prefer: respond-async` when the flag is on. | no (2 sites) | `drop` as a flag — **BB-R-001.4 promises real transaction bundles unconditionally**, and HAPI is atomic by default. Making atomicity opt-in is a Medplum scaling compromise Big Book should not copy. See Contradictions. |
| FF-014 | `validate-terminology` | `fhir/accesspolicy.ts:84` (sets `validateTerminology` on the session `Repository`, declared `fhir/repo.ts:236`); effect at `fhir/repository/validation.ts:115,134-139,183` | Turns on `collect: {tokens}` in validator options; after base + profile validation, calls `validateTerminology(repo, tokens, issues)` and **throws** if any issue is severity `error`. So writes are rejected when a coded field carries a code outside its required ValueSet binding. | no | `defer v0.3` — terminology is BB-R-028/v0.3 (Snowstorm); HAPI's validator has the same capability behind `IValidationSupport` |
| FF-015 | `range-search` | `fhir/repo.ts:2035` (`supportsRangeSearch()`) | Precision-aware range semantics for date/number search parameters (`>`/`>=` against a truncated datetime). **The only feature ORed with a server-wide config key** (`rangeSearch`, CFG-135). Behaviour difference visible at `fhir/search.test.ts:4239-4244`. | no | `drop` — Medplum SQL-builder specific; HAPI implements FHIR date-precision semantics natively |
| FF-016 | `log-streaming` | **none anywhere** — no `log-streaming` and no `logStreaming` outside the CodeSystem, the `.d.ts` union, and `packages/mock/src/mocks/project.ts:29` | Nothing. | **YES — fully dead.** | `drop` |
| FF-017 | `async-batch` | `async-batch.ts:26` | In `asyncBatchHandler`'s middleware, after the `Prefer: respond-async` check: without the flag → `badRequest('Async Batch feature not available')`. With it, the Bundle is parsed, an `AsyncJobExecutor` created and processing queued (legacy vs re-entrant path chosen by `useLegacyBatchProcessing`, PS-030). | no | `defer v0.2` — BB-R-001.7 already defers async batch bundles |

**Dead-flag summary: 5 of 17 are dead** — `aws-comprehend` (FF-003), `billing` (FF-005), `graphql-introspection` (FF-010), `scheduling` (FF-011), `log-streaming` (FF-016). Twelve are live in `packages/server`. Area 2's `graphql-introspection` finding is confirmed and **four more** dead flags are added to it.

---

#### Notes

##### (a) The loader precedence chain, the env name-mangling rule, and BB-R-011.3

**How a key is resolved, exactly.**

1. The server takes **one** config identifier: `runFromCli` passes `argv[2]`, defaulting to `file:medplum.config.json` (`index.ts:61`). There is no `MEDPLUM_CONFIG` environment variable and no implicit env reading.
2. That identifier is split on commas (`loader.ts:49`). Each segment is one source, resolved by prefix (`loader.ts:78-94`):
   - `file:<path>` — `JSON.parse(readFileSync(path))`, path relative to the runtime cwd (`loader.ts:276-278`).
   - `env` — every `MEDPLUM_*` environment variable (`loader.ts:173-268`).
   - `aws:<[region:]ssm-path-prefix>` — `GetParametersByPath` with `WithDecryption: true`, paginated (`cloud/aws/config.ts:17-70`).
   - `gcp:<projectId>:<secretId>` — one GCP Secret Manager secret whose payload is a flat JSON map (`cloud/gcp/config.ts:35-47`).
   - `azure:<vaultHost>:<secretName>` — one Azure Key Vault secret, same flat-map shape (`cloud/azure/config.ts:39-51`).
   - anything else → `throw new Error('Unrecognized config type: ' + configType)`.
3. Segments are loaded **left to right and deep-merged, later wins** (`loader.ts:54-61`). `deepMerge` (`loader.ts:102-121`): objects recurse, **arrays replace wholesale**, primitives overwrite.
4. `baseUrl` is then checked; absent/blank/non-string → throw (`loader.ts:63-65`). This is the **only** required-key check in the whole loader.
5. `addDefaults` fills the ~35 defaulted keys (`config/utils.ts:17-84`), including the auto-generated signing key.
6. `warnInvalidDataWarehouseConfig` logs (never throws) if `dataWarehouse.enabled` is set with bad config (`validate-config.ts:93-102`). There is **no** other validation — no schema, no unknown-key rejection, no type check beyond the env coercion below.

**So: precedence is positional, not source-ranked.** `file:x.json,env` means env wins; `env,file:x.json` means the file wins; `file:x.json` alone never reads any `MEDPLUM_*` variable. Medplum's own `setting-configuration.md` claims "environment variables will take precedence" when both are present — **false** (T38 above).

Within the AWS loader there is a second, internal precedence: the seven `*Secrets` pseudo-keys (CFG-153..159) are resolved **first** into whole objects, then all remaining parameters are applied over them (`cloud/aws/config.ts:41-67`), so `/medplum/prod/database.maxConnections` overrides a value that came from the Secrets Manager blob.

**The env name-mangling rule** (`loader.ts:173-267`), exactly:

1. Skip any variable not starting with `MEDPLUM_`; strip that prefix.
2. Match a **section prefix**, in this order (first match wins) — `DATABASE_`, `READONLY_DATABASE_`, `CACHE_REDIS_`, `RATE_LIMIT_REDIS_`, `PUBSUB_REDIS_`, `BACKGROUND_JOBS_REDIS_`, `REDIS_`, `SMTP_`, `BULLMQ_`, `FISSION_`, `WORKERS_`, `DATA_WAREHOUSE_`. Strip it and target that sub-object. Note `REDIS_` is tested **after** the four qualified redis prefixes, which is why `MEDPLUM_RATE_LIMIT_REDIS_HOST` does not land in `redis`.
3. Inside `DATABASE_` and `READONLY_DATABASE_` only, a further `SSL_` prefix targets `…​.ssl`. **No other nesting is reachable from env** — `capabilityStatement.*`, `workers.bullmq.<worker>`, `arrayColumnPadding.*` and `database.ssl.*` beyond the flat leaves cannot be expressed except as a whole-object JSON string.
4. Lower-case the remainder, then replace `_x` with `X`: `MAX_CONNECTIONS` → `max_connections` → `maxConnections`. This is why `MEDPLUM_DATABASE_MAXCONNECTIONS` silently produces the useless key `maxconnections` — **unknown keys are never rejected**, they are just stored and ignored.
5. Coerce the string by consulting `isIntegerConfig` / `isFloatConfig` / `isBooleanConfig` / `isObjectConfig` / `isArrayConfig` with **both** the dotted path and the bare leaf (`loader.ts:250-264`). Integers → `parseInt`, booleans → `value === 'true'` (so `"1"`, `"yes"`, `"TRUE"` all mean **false**), objects → `JSON.parse` (throws on bad JSON), arrays → comma-split with trim, everything else stays a string.
6. Consequence: a key that is in none of those sets stays a **string**. `heartbeatEnabled`, `saveAuditEvents`, `autoDownloadEnabled`, `dispatchEnabled`, `subscriptionsEnabled`, `preCommitSubscriptionsEnabled`, `vmContextBotsEnabled`, `introspectionEnabled`, `allowProjectSmtp`, `botCustomFunctionsEnabled` (this one *is* listed) — checking `booleanKeys` at `config/utils.ts:185-208` against the boolean keys in `types.ts` shows several booleans **missing from the set**, so `MEDPLUM_HEARTBEAT_ENABLED=false` yields the string `"false"`, which is truthy. This is a real footgun and a direct argument for Spring's typed binding.

**Big Book's Spring equivalent, and whether BB-R-011.3 is achievable.** Yes, and more cheaply than Medplum. Spring Boot's `Environment` already defines the precedence Big Book wants, without any loader code: command-line args → `SPRING_APPLICATION_JSON` → OS environment variables → `application-{profile}.yml` → `application.yml` → `@ConfigurationProperties` defaults. Relaxed binding makes `BIGBOOK_FHIR_BASE_URL` → `bigbook.fhir.base-url` with no hand-written mangling, no missing-from-the-boolean-set bug, and typed binding with JSR-380 validation (`@NotBlank`, `@Positive`) so a bad value fails **at startup**, not on the first request. This matches ARCHITECTURE.md §5's stated order exactly ("Environment variables → Helm values/compose `.env` → defaults in `application.yml`"), so nothing in the architecture needs to change.

What Medplum does that would **not** fit BB-R-011.3, and should be dropped (D32):

- **Cloud-provider secret loaders** (`aws:`, `gcp:`, `azure:`) — three SDK dependencies fetching config at boot. Big Book's answer is Vault in `full` (BIGBOOK stack table) via Spring Cloud Vault, which is an additional `PropertySource`, not a replacement loader, and is absent from `lite` entirely.
- **Comma-composed positional sources** — replaced by Spring profiles (`--spring.profiles.active=lite`), which is also how CFG-062's worker-pool split would be expressed (D41).
- **Config file as the primary source.** BB-R-011.3's "env/values only" means the shipped `application.yml` holds defaults and *nothing environment-specific*; compose `.env` and Helm `values.yaml` supply the rest as env vars. The `lite` install (Note (b)) needs no config file at all.
- **No Docker-secrets loader exists in Medplum** despite the brief's mention — `grep` for `/run/secrets` across `packages/server` returns nothing. Big Book gets this free: Spring Boot reads `/run/secrets/*` via `spring.config.import=configtree:/run/secrets/` in one line, which is the cleanest answer to "secrets never in values files" for compose, and the Kubernetes equivalent is a mounted Secret with the same configtree import.

##### (b) Minimum viable config for a `lite` stack

Given ADR-006 (`lite` = Postgres + Keycloak + Big Book, HAPI embedded, no Redis per D33), the required set collapses hard. Of the 159 Table A rows, **6 must have values** and everything else has a sane default or is dropped.

**Must be supplied (no safe default possible):**

| Big Book property | Why it cannot be defaulted | Medplum analogue |
|---|---|---|
| `bigbook.base-url` | The public origin; it is the token issuer, the `WWW-Authenticate` realm, and the base of every emitted link. Medplum makes it the one hard-fail key. | CFG-002 |
| `spring.datasource.password` | A secret. Compose can generate it into `.env`; Helm must take it from a Secret. | CFG-023 |
| `bigbook.keycloak.admin-password` (bootstrap) | Keycloak's own bootstrap admin, needed for the realm import (BB-R-011.4). | *(no Medplum analogue — Medplum is its own IdP)* |
| `bigbook.bootstrap.admin-email` | Who the super-admin is. Medplum defaults it to `admin@example.com`, which is worse than asking. | CFG-126 |
| `bigbook.bootstrap.admin-password` | **Must have no default** (D34/S1). Generate-and-print-once if unset. | CFG-127 |
| `bigbook.bootstrap.admin-client-secret` | The Appsmith service-account secret (ADR-004, BB-R-013.1). Only required when the `admin` overlay is used, so strictly this is a 5-key core + 1. | CFG-129 |

**Sane defaults, no operator action:** `server.port` 8080 (CFG-001); `spring.datasource.url` → the compose service name (CFG-019..022); `bigbook.app-base-url` → `<base-url>` (CFG-010, and the Appsmith origin only in the overlay); `bigbook.binary.storage` → a DB/local path (CFG-012, BB-R-009 says `lite` uses DB/local); `bigbook.mail.provider` → `none` (CFG-050 — note Medplum's default is `awsses`, which fails at runtime, not boot: S8); `logging.level` → INFO (CFG-011); `bigbook.logging.requests` → **true** (CFG-084 — BB-R-015.1 requires one log line per request carrying project + user, so unlike Medplum this defaults on); `bigbook.cors.allowed-origins` → `<app-base-url>` (CFG-070, **not** `*`); `bigbook.registration.enabled` → **false** (CFG-090); `bigbook.search.max-offset` 10000 (CFG-101); `bigbook.http.max-json-size` 1MB / `max-batch-size` 50MB (CFG-068/069); `bigbook.fhir.max-base64-bytes` 1MB (CFG-105); `server.shutdown=graceful` + 30s (CFG-097); `bigbook.project.default-features` → `[]` (CFG-124).

**Smallest working `lite` config.** Not a file — a compose `.env`, which is exactly what BB-R-011.3 asks for:

```sh
### .env — the whole of lite's configuration
BIGBOOK_BASE_URL=http://localhost:8080/
POSTGRES_PASSWORD=<generated at install>
KEYCLOAK_ADMIN_PASSWORD=<generated at install>
BIGBOOK_BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_EMAIL=admin@example.invalid
BIGBOOK_BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_PASSWORD=<generated; printed once>
### only with the admin overlay:
### BIGBOOK_BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_CLIENT_SECRET=<generated>
```

Five lines, three of them generated by the install script — which is what makes BB-R-011.1's "two commands max" work. Compare Medplum's shipped `medplum.config.json`: 30+ keys including a Redis block, Google and reCAPTCHA credentials, and AWS Lambda settings, none of which a laptop install needs. **Recommendation:** the install script generates `.env` with the three secrets and echoes the admin password once; `docker compose up -d` needs nothing else. A `bigbook.yml` file is supported but never required — which is the strongest form of "configuration by env/values only".

##### (c) Secrets handling

**What Medplum does to keep secrets out of config files:** very little in the open-source path, and quite a lot in the AWS path.

- The **AWS path is the only real story**: SSM parameters with `WithDecryption: true` (`cloud/aws/config.ts:32`) and the seven `*Secrets` pseudo-keys (CFG-153..159) that pull whole credential blobs from Secrets Manager. GCP Secret Manager and Azure Key Vault are equivalents, one flat-JSON secret each.
- **The `file:` path has no protection at all.** The shipped `packages/server/medplum.config.json` contains `database.password`, `redis.password`, a Google OAuth client id, and — notably — `recaptchaSiteKey` and `recaptchaSecretKey` with real-looking Medplum values **committed to the repository** (CFG-066/067). Whatever their status upstream, the pattern is exactly what BB-R-011.3 forbids.
- **`signingKeyPassphrase` is auto-generated per process** when storage is built-in (`config/utils.ts:60-81`), with a logged warning that URLs break across restarts and replicas. It is a convenience, not a secrets-management story.
- **No Docker-secrets support** (no `/run/secrets` reader anywhere).

**What is stored in plaintext in the `Project` resource** — this is the part BB-R-011.3 does *not* address:

- `Project.secret[]` — PS-031..039 plus every bot secret. Plaintext columns in Postgres. **Readable by any project admin** through a plain FHIR read of `Project`: the injected admin policy (`fhir/accesspolicy.ts:341-348`) hides `systemSecret`, `superAdmin` and `strictMode` and makes `systemSetting` readonly, but leaves `setting` and `secret` fully readable and writable. So `smtpPassword` (PS-036) and `OPENAI_API_KEY` (PS-031) are admin-visible by design.
- `Project.systemSecret[]` — hidden from project admins by that same policy, readable by super-admins, and still plaintext at rest.
- Adjacent, from area 1 D36: `ClientApplication.secret` is stored in clear and returned on **every** read, never masked.
- Non-admin members see none of the four fields (all hidden), so the exposure is bounded by admin membership, not by encryption.

**What BB-R-011.3's "secrets never in values files" actually requires**, and what it does not:

1. **Requires:** no secret literal in `values.yaml`, `application.yml`, or any file committed to a chart or repo. Helm takes `existingSecret` references; compose takes `.env` (git-ignored) or `/run/secrets` via `spring.config.import=configtree:/run/secrets/`. Both are one line of Spring config — see (a).
2. **Requires:** the shipped `application.yml` has **no** default for any secret-typed property. CFG-127's `medplum_admin` default is the exact anti-pattern (S1).
3. **Does not cover** at-rest secrets inside the FHIR store. `Project.secret` is a *resource field*, not a values file, and Big Book will copy the shape for wire-compat (BB-R-005.6). **D35: state this explicitly in BB-R-005.6** — in `lite`, `Project.secret` is plaintext in Postgres and readable by project admins, exactly like Medplum; Vault (`full`) is the only profile where it is not. Otherwise the brick reads as a stronger promise than the code can keep.
4. **Suggests a test:** a CI check that greps the chart and compose files for anything matching a password/secret/key property name with a non-empty literal default. Cheap, and it is the only way BB-R-011.3 stays true after ten releases.

##### (d) AWS-specific keys and their cloud-neutral equivalents

Twenty-nine of the 159 rows are AWS-only. `lite` must run on a laptop, so every one of them is `drop` for v0.x.

| Medplum key(s) | What it is for | Big Book / cloud-neutral equivalent |
|---|---|---|
| `awsRegion` (CFG-071) | Region for SES, Lambda, S3, SSM, Secrets Manager, Textract, Comprehend | Nothing. MinIO takes an endpoint + credentials; there is no region concept to configure. |
| `binaryStorage: "s3:<bucket>"` (CFG-012) | S3 blob backend | Same key, different value: `lite` = DB/local, `full` = MinIO via the S3 API (BIGBOOK stack table, BB-R-009). Big Book needs `bigbook.binary.s3.endpoint` + path-style addressing, which Medplum has no key for at all. |
| `sseCustomerKey` (CFG-072) | S3 SSE-C client-supplied encryption key | Drop. MinIO server-side encryption (SSE-S3/SSE-KMS) is configured **on the MinIO server**, not by the client. |
| `signingKey`/`signingKeyId`/`signingKeyPassphrase` (CFG-014..016) | CloudFront signed URLs for S3 storage | MinIO presigned URLs (`minio-java` / AWS SDK v2 presigner) need no separate key pair — they sign with the access key. So the three keys collapse to zero in `full`, and to nothing at all in `lite`. BB-R-009.3/BB-R-027, defer v0.2. |
| `botLambdaRoleArn`, `botLambdaLayerName`, `botCustomFunctionsEnabled`, `defaultBotRuntimeVersion: awslambda` (CFG-073..075, 078) | Lambda bot deployment | Drop. Bots are in-JVM Camel routes (BIGBOOK stack table, BB-R-016). |
| `databaseProxyEndpoint`, `readonlyDatabaseProxyEndpoint` (CFG-038, 040) | RDS Proxy, forcing `ssl.require` | Drop (deprecated upstream too). PgBouncer, if ever needed, is a plain host change plus `disableConnectionConfiguration`-style tuning. |
| `dataWarehouse.destination: s3tables`, `dataWarehouse.awsS3TableArn` (CFG-146, 147) | S3 Tables / Iceberg export | Drop — analytics is an explicit non-goal. |
| `mtlsCertHeader` (CFG-132, `x-amzn-mtls-clientcert*`) | ALB mTLS pass-through header | Drop — mTLS is non-goal v0.x (BB-R-004.7). Traefik's `X-Forwarded-Tls-Client-Cert` would be the equivalent if it ever returns. |
| `aws:` loader + `DatabaseSecrets`, `ReaderDatabaseSecrets`, `RedisSecrets`, `CacheRedisSecrets`, `RateLimitRedisSecrets`, `PubSubRedisSecrets`, `BackgroundJobsRedisSecrets` (CFG-153..159) | SSM + Secrets Manager config loading | Drop. Spring env binding (`lite`) and Spring Cloud Vault (`full`) — see (a). |
| `aiRealtimeTranscriptionUrl` (CFG-134), `aws-textract`/`aws-comprehend` features (FF-003/004), `$ai` + `OPENAI_API_KEY` (FF-001, PS-031) | Managed AI services | Drop — AI is an explicit non-goal. |

The only genuinely *new* configuration Big Book needs that Medplum has no key for is MinIO's S3 endpoint and path-style flag, because Medplum assumes real AWS S3 and reads the endpoint from the SDK's region defaults.

##### (e) Security-relevant keys whose default is unsafe

| # | Key | Default | Why it is unsafe | Big Book must |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | `defaultSuperAdminPassword` (CFG-127) + `defaultSuperAdminEmail` (CFG-126) | **`medplum_admin`** / `admin@example.com`, applied silently with no warning (`seed.ts:63-64`) | A published, guessable super-admin credential on any deployment that does not set the key. Super-admin is the only role that can create projects, override `meta`, and read protected resources. | Have **no default**. If `bigbook.bootstrap.admin-password` is unset, generate a random one, print it once to stdout, and log a warning. (D34, BB-R-005.7.) |
| S2 | `allowedOrigins` (CFG-070) | no `addDefaults` entry, and the shipped dev config sets **`"*"`** | `*` allows any origin to make credentialed cross-origin calls to the FHIR API. Shipped as the copy-paste starting point. | Default to `<app-base-url>` only; `*` requires an explicit opt-in and a startup warning. |
| S3 | `database.ssl.rejectUnauthorized` (CFG-028) | unset (node-pg defaults `true`), **but Medplum's own doc suggests `false`** as a workaround for the 4 KB SSM parameter limit (`server-config.md:884+`) | `false` keeps encryption but stops verifying the server certificate — the connection is MITM-able. The doc presents it as a convenience. | Use `sslmode=verify-full` and never document a "disable verification" workaround. If the CA bundle is awkward, mount it as a file. |
| S4 | `registerEnabled` (CFG-090) | **`true`** (only an explicit `false` disables; `auth/newuser.ts:43`) | With no key set, `/auth/newuser` and `/auth/newproject` are open to the internet — anyone can self-register and create a project on a freshly self-hosted server. `requireVerifiedEmailForProjectCreation` (CFG-091) also defaults off, so there is no second gate. | Default `bigbook.registration.enabled` to **`false`**. The Medplum routes are non-goal (BB-R-004.8a), but Keycloak self-registration is the identical exposure — the realm import must ship with registration disabled (BB-R-011.4). |
| S5 | `introspectionEnabled` (CFG-095) | `false` in `addDefaults`, but the **shipped config sets `true`** | GraphQL schema introspection is expensive and enumerates the whole data model. Shipping it on in the reference config means self-hosters inherit it. BB-R-003.4 wants it togglable; the default matters more than the toggle. | Default **off**, on per project/tenant only. |
| S6 | `autoDownloadEnabled` (CFG-120) | **`true`** (`config/utils.ts:42`) | The server fetches arbitrary `Attachment.url` values found in written resources — an SSRF surface driven by *resource content*, on by default, narrowed only by opt-in per-project prefix lists (PS-009a/b). | `drop` entirely (it is in no brick). If ever wanted: off by default plus a mandatory allow-list. |
| S7 | `allow-dangerous-redirect` project setting (PS-005) | `false` — safe default, but the mechanism is the problem | Enables **prefix** matching of OAuth redirect URIs. Any open redirect or path-traversal under the registered prefix becomes an authorization-code exfiltration path. The name is honest; the capability should not exist. | `drop`. Keycloak's `redirectUris` supports explicit wildcards with per-client review — no blanket prefix mode. |
| S8 | `emailProvider` (CFG-050) | `awsses` when no `smtp` block is present (`config/utils.ts:39`) | Not a vulnerability but a **silent-failure** default: a self-hoster with no AWS credentials gets a server that starts cleanly and then fails every invite, password-reset and verification email at runtime. Invites are how users get in (BB-R-005.4). | Default to `none` and **fail the startup health check** when an email-dependent feature (invite, verify-email required action) is enabled without a transport. BB-R-011.5 health endpoints are the right place. |
| S9 | `allowUnsafeOutbound` (CFG-136) | `false` — safe default, but all-or-nothing | Turning it on to reach one service on the compose network also permits plain HTTP and unsafe hostnames everywhere, for rest-hooks, bots and external-IdP calls alike. `lite` genuinely needs *some* private-network access (n8n, mail catcher). | Replace with an allow-list: `bigbook.outbound.allowed-private-cidrs` (D37, ~40 glue lines), so `lite` reaches the compose network without disabling SSRF protection globally. |
| S10 | `vmContextBotsEnabled` (CFG-076) | `false` in `addDefaults`, **`true` in the shipped config** | Node's `vm` module is explicitly **not a security sandbox**. Enabling it runs project-authored code inside the server process, with the server's network position and file descriptors. | `drop` — Big Book bots are compiled Camel routes in the image, never runtime-uploaded code (BB-R-016). |
| S11 | `bcryptHashSalt` (CFG-093) | `10` | Cost 10 is below current guidance (OWASP suggests ≥10 for bcrypt but recommends Argon2id outright). Not a Big Book problem, but worth recording as a reason not to build an own credential store. | `→ Keycloak` — Argon2 is the KC 24+ default; no Big Book key. |

---

#### Contradictions with REQUIREMENTS.md/ADRs

**BB-R-011.3** — *"Configuration by env/values only; every key documented in one table (`docs/guides/config.md`), with defaults; secrets never in values files."*

- **"env/values only" — achievable, and Medplum is the cautionary tale, not the model.** Medplum has five loaders selected by a single CLI argument with positional precedence (Note (a)); the `env` source is opt-in and its name mangling is hand-rolled, lossy (only two nesting levels reachable), untyped for several boolean keys, and silently ignores misspellings. Spring's native property resolution gives Big Book the ARCHITECTURE.md §5 order for free. **No contradiction — but the brick should name Spring's order explicitly so "env/values only" is not read as "we will write a loader".**
- **"every key documented in one table, with defaults"** — Medplum documents ~90 of ~159 flattened keys, misses most nested tuning keys, documents two keys that **do not exist** (`auditEventLogGroup`, `auditEventLogStream`, T39), and states a precedence rule that is **false** (T38). A hand-maintained table drifts. **Recommend** generating `docs/guides/config.md` from the `@ConfigurationProperties` classes and failing CI on an undocumented property (T40).
- **"secrets never in values files"** — achievable and cheap (configtree / `existingSecret`), **but it does not cover `Project.secret`**, which is plaintext at rest and admin-readable in Medplum and will be in Big Book too. Code reality: `fhir/accesspolicy.ts:341-348` hides `systemSecret` from project admins but leaves `secret` readable and writable; `email/utils.ts:37` reads `smtpPassword` straight out of it. **D35: BB-R-005.6 must say so, or it over-promises.**
- Also: the shipped `medplum.config.json` contains real-looking `recaptchaSecretKey` and `redis.password` values in the repository. Whatever their upstream status, Big Book's shipped `application.yml` must have **no secret-typed default at all** (S1).

**BB-R-005.6** — *"Project settings (v0.1 subset): `checkReferencesOnWrite`, `defaultProfile`, `features[]`, `defaultPatientAccessPolicy`, `setting[]`/`secret[]` (secrets in Keycloak client attrs or env for `lite`; Vault in `full`), `rateLimit` (→ BB-R-023, v0.2)."*

- **`rateLimit` is `systemSetting`, not `setting`** (`ratelimit.ts:50`, read `:144-146`) — super-admin-only, and it is one of **seven** rate/quota system settings, not one: `rateLimit`, `authRateLimit`, `loginRateLimit`, `userFhirQuota`, `totalFhirQuota`, `enableFhirQuota`, `resourceCap` (+`enableResourceCap`). Area 1 T9 already flagged the field; this area supplies the full list and the read sites (PS-015..022).
- **The brick's list is not the runtime-read list.** `checkReferencesOnWrite`, `defaultProfile` and `defaultPatientAccessPolicy` are **top-level `Project` fields**, not `setting[]` entries — mixing them into a "settings subset" conflates two storage shapes. The actual runtime-read `Project.setting` names are the fourteen in Table B rows PS-001..PS-014.
- **`secret[]` "in Keycloak client attrs or env for `lite`"** contradicts wire-compat: Big Book will expose `Project.secret` on the `Project` resource (BB-R-014.3, v0.2), which means it is in Postgres like Medplum's, whatever backs it. Keycloak client attributes are also plaintext and admin-readable, so the phrasing promises a protection it does not deliver. See D35.
- **Missing from the subset, and needed in v0.1:** `mfaRequired` (PS-001) is required by BB-R-004.3; `allowedPractitionerEmailDomain` (PS-007) is the GAP area 1 flagged at ADM-011.

**BB-R-004.3** — *"MFA (TOTP) per user; project can require MFA at invite (`mfaRequired`) — Keycloak required action, set by BB-R-005 invite."*

- Code reality (`auth/utils.ts:129`, PS-001): `mfaRequired` is **not only an invite-time flag**. It is `Project.setting[mfaRequired].valueBoolean OR User.mfaRequired`, evaluated at **every password login**, and the project value **wins over** a user-level `false` — a project cannot grant an exception. Users who have not enrolled are forced to enroll at their next login, including **existing** users. The brick's "at invite" framing understates it: flipping the project setting retroactively gates everyone.
- **Two sibling settings are missing from the brick:** `allowedMfaMethods` (PS-002 — `totp`/`email`; area 1 D38 correctly makes email OTP non-goal) and `appName` (PS-003), which white-labels the TOTP authenticator issuer and the MFA email. Without `appName`, every Big Book tenant's authenticator entry says whatever the realm display name says — a visible branding gap in a multi-tenant product (T41).
- Keycloak's mapping is a **conditional-OTP authentication flow bound per organisation**, not a per-user required action, if the "project can require it" semantics are to be preserved. Area 1 D38 already puts this at v0.2 — consistent, but BB-R-004.3 still says v0.1.

**BB-R-023** — *"Rate limits per project/user — wire Traefik — v0.2."*

- Code reality: Medplum has **two different mechanisms**, and Traefik covers only one.
  1. **HTTP rate limits** — three categories (`login` 5/min, `auth` 1200/min *documented* but `160` actually applied, `default` 60000/min), keyed by **IP address** (`ratelimit.ts:116-120`), Redis-backed, overridable per project by PS-015..017. Traefik's `RateLimit` middleware does this.
  2. **FHIR interaction quota** — cost-weighted per interaction (READ 1, HISTORY 10, SEARCH 20, WRITE 100), enforced per **membership** and per **project**, with an async variant that *delays* rather than rejects (`asyncDelayScaling`, CFG-116). Plus a **resource cap** (PS-021, resources created per project per 24h). **Traefik cannot express any of this** — it has no notion of a per-request cost or a per-tenant token bucket keyed on a JWT claim.
- So BB-R-023's single "wire Traefik" fill is **insufficient for the second mechanism**. Either drop cost-weighted quotas and resource caps as non-goals, or accept that they are glue (D36). Also note the keying difference: Medplum limits **by IP**, so a whole clinic behind one NAT shares a bucket; Traefik would do the same unless keyed on the project claim.
- Two code-level oddities for the parity guide: `defaultAuthRateLimit`'s `addDefaults` value (`160`) makes `ratelimit.ts`'s stated category default (`1200`) unreachable (CFG-113), and `defaultRateLimit: -1` is a magic sentinel that disables the limiter entirely (CFG-112).

**BB-R-003.4** — *"Introspection togglable per project (`graphql-introspection` feature); depth/search-count limits configurable."*

- **The `graphql-introspection` feature flag is dead** (FF-010) — zero read sites in `packages/server`, `packages/core`, `packages/app` or `packages/fhir-router`; only test fixtures and mocks. Introspection is controlled **server-wide** by `introspectionEnabled` (CFG-095) → `FhirRouter` option → `fhir-router/src/graphql/graphql.ts:127-128`. The per-project check was removed by commit `a54b65933` "FHIR Router (#1435)". Medplum's own docs still list the flag as working. **Area 2's finding is confirmed, and the brick is wrong about Medplum: "togglable per project" is not what Medplum does.** Big Book can still choose per-tenant toggling, but should say so as a deliberate improvement rather than as parity.
- The "depth/search-count limits configurable" half **is** real and **is** per project — `graphqlMaxDepth` (PS-026), `graphqlMaxSearches` (PS-027), plus `graphqlBatchedSearchSize` (PS-028) — all `systemSetting`, super-admin-only. HAPI's GraphQL provider has no equivalent knobs, so these are **glue**, not wire. The brick's `Fill: wire HAPI GraphQL` understates it.

**BB-R-001.4** — *"Batch (`type: batch`) and transaction (`type: transaction`) bundles…"* (unconditional)

- Code reality (FF-013): in Medplum, **a `transaction` Bundle is only atomic if the project holds the `transaction-bundles` feature**. Without it, `fhir-router/src/batch.ts:874` degrades transactions to batch semantics — no DB transaction, no rollback, no serialization-failure retry. Medplum's own docs describe this under "transaction limits". Big Book's brick promises transactions unconditionally, and HAPI is atomic by default. **No change needed to the brick** — but the parity guide must record that Medplum's default behaviour differs, or a wire-compat test written against a Medplum project without the flag will "pass" for the wrong reason.

**ADR-006 implications** (`lite` = Postgres + Keycloak + Big Book; HAPI embedded)

- **Redis is a hard dependency of Medplum's server** — `initRedis` is unconditional (`redis.ts:44-47`), and rate limiting, every BullMQ queue, websockets, FHIRcast, the server registry and the resource cache all require it. Five config objects (CFG-041..049) exist to tune and split it. ADR-006's three containers leave no room for it, so all five are `drop` in v0.1 (D33). This is only sound because BB-R-023 (rate limits) is v0.2 and Traefik-based, BB-R-007.8 (websockets) is deferred, and HAPI's subscription delivery is DB-backed rather than queue-backed. **If any of those three change, ADR-006's container count is back in play** — worth one line in the ADR's Consequences.
- **`workers.enabled` and the two shipped partial config files** (CFG-062, D41) are Medplum's documented way to split an HTTP pool from a worker pool. ADR-006 puts everything in one JVM, so the whole mechanism drops for `lite`. The `full` equivalent is a Spring profile, not a config key — consistent with BB-R-011.2's `profile: lite|full`.
- **HAPI-embedded shifts ~40 of Table A's keys from "Big Book config" to "HAPI/Spring config"** (all of `database.*`, `port`, `logLevel`, `shutdownTimeoutMilliseconds`, `keepAliveTimeout`, `accurateCountThreshold`, `bcryptHashSalt`…). BB-R-011.3's "one table" must therefore document **Spring/HAPI properties alongside `bigbook.*` ones**, or half the surface an operator touches will be undocumented. That is a real scope note for `docs/guides/config.md`: it is not a table of Big Book's own properties, it is a table of everything an operator sets.
- **ADR-006 removes the `signingKey` problem** (CFG-014..016): with HAPI embedded and `lite` storage in the database, there are no presigned URLs to sign in v0.1, so Medplum's auto-generated-throwaway-key warning has no Big Book analogue until BB-R-009.3/BB-R-027 land in v0.2 with MinIO (whose presigner needs no separate key pair — see (d)).

---

## Area 5 — MedplumClient surface (draft)

### 5.1 Public methods, client-side behaviours, and the SDK-grade contract


Source snapshot: medplum `packages/core/src` at repo `main` (client.ts 4993 lines, read in full). `Source` paths in this area are relative to `packages/core/src` unless stated. BB-R mapping is against REQUIREMENTS.md / ADR-003 as of big-book `a1b9f49`.

Conventions follow MEDPLUM-INVENTORY.md §Conventions. Two extra column meanings for this area:

- **Server contract it depends on** — the definition of *SDK-grade C*: what Big Book must return for this method to work against `@medplum/core` unpatched. "none beyond FHIR R4" means the method needs nothing Medplum-specific.
- **Java SDK disposition** — `port` (v0.1 BB-R-012), `port v0.2`, `skip (reason)`, `n/a local helper`.

Cross-references to areas 1–4 are by their IDs (ADM-*, D*, T*); this area does not re-derive their findings.

---
