---

#### Table B — client-side behaviours that constrain the server

| ID | Behaviour | Source (file:line) | What it does | Server implication | Java SDK disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SDC-001 | **Auto-batching** | `client.ts:1100-1106` (opt-in), `:1363-1379` (enqueue), `:3942-3994` (flush) | Only when `autoBatchTime > 0`. Any `get()` whose URL starts with `fhirBaseUrl` and that does not set `disableAutoBatch` is queued instead of dispatched; a `setTimeout(autoBatchTime)` flushes. A **single** queued entry is dispatched as a normal request. Two or more are packed into `Bundle{type:'batch', entry:[{request:{method:'GET', url:<path relative to fhirBaseUrl>}}]}` and `POST`ed to the FHIR base. Responses are unpacked **strictly by index**: `response.entry[i]` ↔ `entries[i]`; an entry whose `response.outcome` fails `isOk()` rejects that caller, otherwise `entry.resource` resolves it. | Server must (a) accept a `batch` Bundle at the FHIR base, (b) return entries **in request order** — index correlation, no `fullUrl` matching, (c) put an `OperationOutcome` in `entry.response.outcome` for failures whose `id` slug is one of `ok`/`created`/`not-modified`/`accepted` for success (`outcomes.ts:437-441`) — a HAPI batch response with **no** `outcome` on success passes `isOk` vacuously and resolves with `entry.resource`, which is correct; but a HAPI **failure** entry with no `outcome` would silently resolve `undefined` instead of rejecting. Default is **off** (`autoBatchTime` default 0), so this never fires unless the app opts in. | `skip` — see note (c) |
| SDC-002 | **Read/request cache** | `:1092-1098` (construction), `:3637-3705` (get/set/delete), `:1968` (getCached) | An `LRUCache<ReadablePromise>` of `resourceCacheSize` (default 1000) entries. `cacheTime` defaults to **60 000 ms in a browser and 0 (disabled) in Node** (`:1092-1093`, `:117-119`). Keys: the plain URL for reads, and `'search-'`/`'searchOne-'`/`'searchResources-'`/`'{type}-requestSchema'` prefixes for the others. Entries expire by `requestTime + cacheTime < now`. Caching is **disabled entirely** for any request carrying an `x-medplum-on-behalf-of` header (`:3640`). An aborted request's entry is evicted (`:3672-3676`). | Server must tag summary/partial results with `meta.tag.code = 'SUBSETTED'` or they poison the read cache (`:3688`). Nothing else. | `n/a local helper` — optional Caffeine cache; default it **off** (matching Node semantics) |
| SDC-003 | **Refresh lock (cross-tab Web Locks) and refresh grace period** | `:4234-4244` (`refreshIfExpired`), `:4261-4272` (`refresh`), `:4282-4320` (`runRefreshWithLock`) | `refreshIfExpired` fires a refresh when `accessTokenExpires` is set and `isAuthenticated(grace)` is false. `refreshGracePeriod` defaults to **300 000 ms (5 min)** (`:120`, `:1087`) — the client refreshes 5 minutes *before* expiry. `refresh()` is single-flight via `this.refreshPromise`. When `navigator.locks` exists, the network call runs inside a Web Lock named `medplum-refresh:{storage.makeKey('activeLogin')}` so sibling tabs serialize; on acquiring the lock a tab re-reads `activeLogin` from storage and skips the network call if a peer already minted a newer token. Order of preference inside the lock: adopt a peer token → `refresh_token` grant → `client_credentials` re-login (`:4298-4310`). | **Access-token lifetime must exceed the grace period.** A Keycloak default of 5 minutes (300 s) makes `isAuthenticated()` false the instant the token is issued, producing a refresh on every single request. Big Book must set the realm access-token lifespan to **≥ 15 min**, or document lowering `refreshGracePeriod`. Refresh tokens must be reusable (or rotation must be tolerated by the single-flight lock). | `port` behaviour, `skip` mechanism — a JVM client uses a `ReentrantLock`/`AtomicReference<CompletableFuture>` per client instance; there is no cross-process equivalent and none is needed |
| SDC-004 | **401-retry-once flow** | `:3727-3730`, `:4148-4158`, `MAX_AUTH_ATTEMPTS = 2` at `:827` | Any `401` calls `handleUnauthenticated`. Attempt 0 → forced `refresh(undefined, true)` (bypassing the local expiry check, since the server rejected a token that looks locally valid) then replays the request with `authAttempt: 1`. A `401` on the replay is **terminal**: `clear()`, `onUnauthenticated?.()`, throw `OperationOutcomeError(unauthorized)`. Never recurses. | Server must return `401` (not 403) for expired/invalid bearer tokens, and the refresh must actually produce a working token — otherwise the user is logged out after exactly two attempts. `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer realm="<baseUrl>"` is emitted by Medplum (area 1 ADM-002) but the SDK does not read it. | `port` — same one-retry bound |
| SDC-005 | **Retry/backoff on 429 and 5xx** | `:3796-3846` (`fetchWithRetry`), `:3909-3918` (`getRetryDelay`), `:4991-4993` (`isRetryable`) | Retryable = `status === 429 || status >= 500`. Default `maxRetries: 2`, `maxRetryTime: 2000 ms`. Delay = `500 * 1.5^attempt` (500, 750, 1125 ms), raised to `secondsUntilReset * 1000` for any rate limit whose `remainingUnits` is 0. **If the computed delay exceeds `maxRetryTime` the client gives up and returns the error response immediately** — so with the 2 s default, a `RateLimit` header advertising more than 2 s of reset time means zero retries. Network errors (`Failed to fetch`) also retry, and the first one dispatches an `offline` event. `AbortError` is never retried. | Emitting a `RateLimit` header with a long reset actively *disables* retries at default settings. Prefer omitting it, or keep resets short. `5xx` must be genuinely transient — the client will repeat non-idempotent `POST`s. | `port` — same policy; make `maxRetries`/`maxRetryTime` configurable |
| SDC-006 | **`X-Medplum: extended`** | `:4031-4033` (every request), `:2659-2661` (XHR upload path) | Set on **every** request unless `extendedMode === false`. Default is on (`:399`). | Cross-reference **area 1 D4** and **area 2**: responses must carry `meta.project`, `meta.author`, `meta.compartment`. Also **T14**: `meta.compartment` must contain `Project/<id>` or the SDK's client-side compartment check in `access.ts` never matches. Not re-derived here. | `port` — send the header by default; expose `extendedMode(false)` |
| SDC-007 | **`Prefer: respond-async` / `startAsyncRequest` / `pollStatus`** | `:3570-3578`, `:3759-3765`, `:3920-3937`, `:4909-4934` | `startAsyncRequest` forces `POST` + `Prefer: respond-async`. On `202` **and** `pollStatusOnAccepted`, the status URL comes from `Content-Location` → `Location` → `OperationOutcome.issue[0].diagnostics` (the CORS fallback). The first poll is immediate with `Prefer` stripped; subsequent polls wait `pollStatusPeriod` (default 1000 ms). Polls set `redirect: 'follow'`. There is **no poll count limit** — an `AsyncJob` stuck in `accepted` polls forever unless the caller passes an `AbortSignal`. `followRedirectOnOk` / `followRedirectOnCreated` do the same lookup on 200/201. | Cross-reference **area 1 ADM-004**: `202` + `Content-Location: {baseUrl}fhir/R4/job/{id}/status` + an `accepted` OperationOutcome. The status URL must return `202` while running and the **final payload** (not a wrapper) when done. HAPI's batch2 job status URLs differ in both path and body — this is the v0.2 glue. | `port v0.2` |
| SDC-008 | **`ClientStorage` and what is persisted** | `storage.ts:22-70`; keys written at `client.ts:1269` (`activeLogin` clear), `:3189` (`activeLogin`), `:3243` (`logins`), `:4168` (`pkceState`), `:4171` (`codeVerifier`) | Backed by `globalThis.localStorage` when present, else an in-memory `Map`. Optional `storagePrefix` namespaces keys (and makes `clear()` prefix-scoped rather than nuking the whole store). Exactly **four** keys: `activeLogin` (a `LoginState`: project ref, profile ref, access token, refresh token), `logins` (an array of `LoginState`, deduped by profile reference), `pkceState`, `codeVerifier`. Note `logins` and `activeLogin` hold **plaintext refresh tokens in `localStorage`**. | None — purely client-side. | `skip` the browser backing; `port` the interface: `ClientStorage` becomes a `TokenStore` SPI with an in-memory default and (v0.2) a file-backed impl for a CLI. **Do not** default to a file store: writing refresh tokens to disk without the user asking is worse on a server than in a browser. |
| SDC-009 | **Cross-tab storage listener** | `:4754-4781` | A `window` `storage` listener. `key === null` (a full `clear()` from another tab) → `location.reload()`. A change to `activeLogin` whose `profile.reference` differs, or that disagrees with the in-memory session, → `location.reload()`; otherwise adopts the new tokens in place. | None. | `skip` — no analogue |
| SDC-010 | **Invalidation after writes** | `:2334` (create), `:2396-2397` (createIfNoneExist), `:2430-2432` (upsert), `:2816-2818` (update), `:2862-2864` (patch), `:2887-2888` (delete), `:1303-1310` (`invalidateSearches`) | Every write invalidates **all cached searches for that resource type** (`invalidateSearches` scans cache keys for `endsWith(typeUrl)` or `includes(typeUrl + '?')`), invalidates the `_history` URL, and (except delete) writes the server's response into the read cache. `post`/`put`/`patch`/`delete` also invalidate the exact target URL (`:1405`, `:1429`, `:1450`, `:1469`). | None. It does mean a write must return the full resource (SDK-064/066) or the cache is populated with `undefined`. | `n/a local helper` — only relevant if the Java client caches |
| SDC-011 | **`EventTarget` events the client emits** | `MedplumClientEventMap` at `:955-963`; dispatch sites `:1117` / `:1132` (storage), `:1277` (clear), `:3259` + `:3262` / `:3267` (profile), `:3711-3715` (resourceModified), `:3835` (offline) | Seven events: `change` (active login or profile identity changed), `offline` (first `Failed to fetch` of a retry series), `profileRefreshing`, `profileRefreshed`, `storageInitialized`, `storageInitFailed{error}`, `resourceModified{resourceType, operation, id?, resource?}`. `resourceModified` is **only dispatched when a listener is registered** (`:3712`). Conditional writes emit even when the server changed nothing, except on a real `304`. | None. | `skip` as an `EventTarget`; the JVM shape is a `List<BigBookClientListener>` or an `ApplicationEventPublisher` in the Spring starter (BB-R-012.7). `resourceModified` is worth keeping; the rest are browser lifecycle noise. |
| SDC-012 | **`fhirUrl` construction and `isInternalUrl`** | `:1075-1081` (all six URLs), `:1732-1734`, `:4005-4021`, `:3797-3799` | `baseUrl` is forced to a trailing slash; `fhirBaseUrl = concatUrls(baseUrl, fhirUrlPath ?? 'fhir/R4')`; `authorizeUrl`/`tokenUrl`/`logoutUrl`/`fhircastHubUrl`/`cdsServicesUrl` all default to `baseUrl + <path>` and each may be overridden **absolutely**. Relative URLs passed to `get`/`post` are resolved against `baseUrl` at dispatch (`:3797`). `isInternalUrl` decides whether to attach credentials: relative URLs are internal; absolute ones must share the origin **and** be under the `baseUrl` path prefix. | **Big Book's FHIR base must be reachable at `{baseUrl}fhir/R4/`** (or the SDK user must set `fhirUrlPath`). Auth endpoints must be same-origin-and-under-path with the API base, or the bearer token is silently dropped — this rules out pointing `tokenUrl` straight at a Keycloak on a different host without also setting `defaultHeaders`. Reinforces ADR-003's "token issuer is the Big Book public URL". | `port` — same URL algebra |
| SDC-013 | **`MedplumClientOptions` — every option** | `:134-413` | `baseUrl` (`https://api.medplum.com/`; forced trailing slash; must start with http) · `authorizeUrl` (`{base}oauth2/authorize`) · `tokenUrl` (`{base}oauth2/token`) · `logoutUrl` (`{base}oauth2/logout`) · `fhirUrlPath` (`fhir/R4/`) · `fhircastHubUrl` (`fhircast/STU3`) · `cdsServicesUrl` (`{base}cds-services`) · `clientId` (`''`) · `clientSecret` (`''`) · `accessToken` (none; when set, skips resume-from-storage) · `authCredentialsMethod` (`'body'`; `'header'` moves client creds to HTTP Basic at the token endpoint) · `resourceCacheSize` (1000) · `cacheTime` (60 000 browser / **0 Node**; 0 disables the cache object entirely) · `autoBatchTime` (**0 = off**) · `maxRetryTime` (2000 ms) · `maxRetries` (2) · `refreshGracePeriod` (300 000 ms) · `fetch` (`globalThis.fetch`; **throws at construction if absent**) · `storage` (`ClientStorage` over `localStorage` or memory) · `createPdf` (none; `createPdf()` throws without it) · `onUnauthenticated` (no-op; called on terminal 401 and on a failed token fetch) · `redirect` (`RequestRedirect`, default follow-nothing) · `verbose` (**deprecated** → `logLevel`) · `logLevel` (`'none'`) · `extendedMode` (**true**) · `defaultHeaders` (`{}`; applied first, before Accept/X-Medplum/Authorization) · `storagePrefix` (`''`). Per-request `MedplumRequestOptions` adds `followRedirectOnOk`, `followRedirectOnCreated`, `pollStatusOnAccepted`, `pollStatusPeriod` (1000), `maxRetries`, `maxRetryTime`, `disableAutoBatch`, `duplex`. | The only options that change what the *server* must do are `extendedMode` (SDC-006), `fhirUrlPath` (SDC-012), `authCredentialsMethod` (server must accept both Basic and form-body client auth at the token endpoint) and `refreshGracePeriod` (SDC-003). | `port` the subset that is not browser-specific: `baseUrl`, `authorizeUrl`, `tokenUrl`, `logoutUrl`, `fhirUrlPath`, `clientId`, `clientSecret`, `accessToken`, `authCredentialsMethod`, `maxRetries`, `maxRetryTime`, `refreshGracePeriod`, `extendedMode`, `defaultHeaders`, `logLevel`, `tokenStore`. `skip`: `fhircastHubUrl`, `cdsServicesUrl`, `createPdf`, `storagePrefix`, `autoBatchTime`, `verbose`, `redirect`. |
| SDC-014 | **Error handling and `OperationOutcome.id` slugs** | `outcomes.ts:8-25` (slug constants), `:437-534` (predicates, `getStatus`, `OperationOutcomeError`), `client.ts:3767-3768`, `:4627-4653`, `:3963`, `:3988-3989` | Every response ≥400 becomes `throw new OperationOutcomeError(normalizeOperationOutcome(body))`; a non-OperationOutcome body is wrapped in `badRequest(<stringified>)`. The predicates `isOk`/`isCreated`/`isAccepted`/`isRedirect`/`isNotFound`/`isConflict`/`isGone`/`isUnauthenticated` and `getStatus()` switch **purely on `OperationOutcome.id`**, never on `issue.code`. Full slug set: `ok`, `created`, `accepted`, `found`, `not-modified`, `unauthorized`, `forbidden`, `not-found`, `conflict`, `gone`, `precondition-failed`, `multiple-matches`, `content-too-large`, `unsupported-media-type`, `business-rule`, `too-many-requests`, `server-timeout`. Fallback for an unrecognised id: 500 if `issue[0].code === 'exception'`, else **400**. The token endpoint has its own error path: an `OperationOutcome` body → rethrown; an OAuth2 `{error_description}` → `badRequest`; anything else → a raw `Error` with the JSON stringified (`:4627-4645`). | **Big Book must set `OperationOutcome.id` to these exact slugs** — confirms area 1 **D4** and `medplum-parity.md`. HAPI sets no `id`, so every HAPI error reads as HTTP 400 to `getStatus()` and `isGone()`/`isNotFound()` always return false. Two slugs are missing from the known lists: `multiple-matches` (412, on an ambiguous conditional update — `upsertResource` depends on it) and `business-rule` (422→ which `getStatus` maps to 422, contradicting BB-R-014.6's "422→400 on validation" — see contradiction C6). Also: a `429` **must** carry an OperationOutcome or the client wraps the HTML error page text into a `badRequest`. | `port` — a `BigBookOperationOutcomeException` with the same slug switch |
| SDC-015 | **Token verification on every token fetch** | `:4661-4699` (`fetchTokens`), `:4708-4738` (`verifyTokens`) | Form-encodes the params; adds `Authorization: Basic` if `basicAuth` is set or `credentialsInHeader` and both client creds are present (deleting them from the body); sends `credentials: 'include'`. **A non-`ok` response clears the active login, calls `onUnauthenticated`, and throws.** On success: if the access token is a JWT it checks `exp` (throws `unauthorizedTokenExpired`, clears login) and the audience via `cid` — falling back to `client_id` — against the configured `clientId` (throws `unauthorizedTokenAudience`, clears login). Then `setActiveLogin({accessToken, refreshToken, project: tokens.project, profile: tokens.profile})`. | Confirms **D3** precisely: the token **body** must carry `project` and `profile` references; the token **claims** must carry `exp` and either `cid` or `client_id` matching the client, plus `login_id`. Note `verifyTokens` does **not** validate the signature against JWKS despite the doc comment at `:4703`. | `port` — but a JVM client *should* validate the signature against JWKS |
| SDC-016 | **WebSocket subscription manager** | `subscriptions/index.ts` (`SubscriptionManager`); constants at `subscriptions/constants.ts` | Lives in `subscriptions/`, instantiated lazily by `getSubscriptionManager()` against `getWebSocketUrl(baseUrl, '/ws/subscriptions-r4')`. Per criteria: create a `Subscription` with `channel.type = 'websocket'`, fetch a binding token via `$get-ws-binding-token`, send a `bind` frame. Refcounted per `(criteria, props)` with a 10 s unref grace (`UNREF_GRACE_PERIOD_MS`). Tokens are re-fetched 5 min before expiry, polled every 60 s. Ping interval 5 s. Rebinds on `change` (profile switch) and pauses token refresh entirely while `getProfile()` is falsy. | `/ws/subscriptions-r4` + `Subscription/$get-ws-binding-token` returning `Parameters{token, websocket-url, expiration}`. Both are Medplum-specific; HAPI's WebSocket subscription handshake is `bind <subscription-id>` on `/websocket` with no token. This is BB-R-025 and the shapes do **not** line up. | `skip v0.1` / `port v0.2` under BB-R-025 |

---

#### Notes

##### (a) The SDK-grade C contract, consolidated

An ordered checklist. Each item names the `client.ts` line that proves it. **[confirmed]** = areas 1–4 already found it and this area confirms; **[new]** = not previously recorded; **[uncovered]** = no Big Book brick covers it today.

**Phase 1 — the client must be able to mint a token.**

1. `POST {baseUrl}oauth2/token` accepts form-encoded `grant_type=client_credentials` with `client_id`/`client_secret` **in the body** *and* as HTTP Basic (`:4662-4675`). **[confirmed — BB-R-004.8]**
2. The token response body is JSON containing `access_token`, `refresh_token`, **`project: {reference:'Project/…'}`** and **`profile: {reference:'Practitioner/…'}`** (`:575-583`, `:4732-4737`). Medplum also emits `token_type`, `expires_in`, `scope`, `id_token`, `patient`, `smart_style_url` (`server/src/oauth/token.ts:956-970`); the SDK reads only the four. **[confirmed — D3]**
3. The access token is a **JWT** whose payload carries `exp` (`:4715`, `jwt.ts:104-114`). Without it `isAuthenticated()` is permanently false and `refreshIfExpired` never fires. **[new — D3 covers the claims but not the "must be a JWT at all" consequence]**
4. The JWT carries **`cid`** or **`client_id`** equal to the configured `clientId` (`:4720-4729`). A mismatch throws `unauthorizedTokenAudience` and wipes the login. Keycloak emits `azp`, not `client_id`, on user tokens — a mapper is required. **[confirmed — D3]**
5. The JWT carries a **`login_id`** claim (`jwt.ts:87-94`). This is the gate: without it `medplumServer` is false, `refreshProfile()` returns `undefined` at `:3247`, `/auth/me` is never called, and `getProfile()` stays undefined forever — the client silently behaves as signed-out with a perfectly valid token. **[confirmed — D3]**
6. A non-`ok` token response must carry either an `OperationOutcome` or an OAuth2 `{error, error_description}` body (`:4627-4645`). **[new]**
7. Access-token lifetime must exceed `refreshGracePeriod` (300 s default) or every request triggers a refresh (`:3211-3216`, `:4237`). Practical floor: **15 minutes**. **[new — uncovered]**

**Phase 2 — the client must be able to load the session.**

8. `GET {baseUrl}auth/me` (sent with `cache: no-cache`) returns `{project, membership, profile, config, accessPolicy}` (`:902-908`, `:3252-3256`). `profile` must have an `id` (dereferenced unguarded at `:3255`). **[confirmed — BB-R-004.8, ADR-003 option B]**
9. `project` must include `id`, `name` (used to self-heal the stored login display, `:3283`), `superAdmin` (`:3310`), and for the app `features`/`strictMode`. **[confirmed]**
10. `membership` must include `id`, `profile`, `admin` (`:3319`). **[confirmed]**
11. `config` is a `UserConfiguration` **with an `id`** (`:906`). **[confirmed — D4]**
12. `accessPolicy` is the **compiled** policy — parameters already substituted, admin rules already injected — because `access.ts` evaluates it client-side. **[confirmed — D4]**

**Phase 3 — the client must be able to do FHIR.**

13. FHIR base at `{baseUrl}fhir/R4/` (`:1076`). **[confirmed — BB-R-014.1]**
14. Every request carries `X-Medplum: extended` (`:4032`) ⇒ responses need `meta.project`, `meta.author`, `meta.compartment` (with `Project/<id>`). **[confirmed — D4, T14]**
15. Every request carries `Accept: application/fhir+json, */*; q=0.1` (`:114`, `:4029`); responses must have a `content-type` containing `json` or the body is read as text and, on ≥400, wrapped into a `badRequest` (`:3737-3738`, `:3790`). **[new]**
16. Every request sets `credentials: 'include'` for internal URLs (`:4047-4049`) ⇒ **CORS must allow credentials** and echo the origin, not `*`. **[new — uncovered]**
17. Writes return the **full resource body**, not a bare `Location` (`:2339`, `:2816`). **[new]**
18. Error responses carry `OperationOutcome` with the exact **`id` slug** from the 17-slug set (`outcomes.ts:8-25`). Missing from `medplum-parity.md` per the D4 note: **`multiple-matches`** (412) and **`business-rule`** (422). **[confirmed + 2 new]**
19. Search results emit `Bundle.link[relation='next']` with an absolute URL, and honour `_count=1000` (`:1933-1943`). **[new]**
20. `_summary`/`_elements`-narrowed resources carry `meta.tag.code = 'SUBSETTED'` (`:3688`) or they poison the read cache. **[new — uncovered]**
21. `_offset` is honoured on `_history` (`:2228`). **[new — uncovered]**
22. Conditional create (`If-None-Exist` header) and conditional update (`PUT {type}?{query}`) are supported (`:2392`, `:2421`). **[confirmed — BB-R-002]**
23. `POST /fhir/R4` accepts batch **and** transaction Bundles, returning entries in request order (`:2992`, `:3985-3993`). **[confirmed — BB-R-012.4]**
24. `POST /fhir/R4/$graphql` with `{query, operationName, variables}` (`:3094`). **[confirmed — BB-R-012.4]**
25. `POST /fhir/R4/{type}/$validate` accepts a **bare resource** body (`:2913`). **[new]**
26. `POST /fhir/R4/Binary` accepts a raw body, honours `?_filename=` and `X-Security-Context:`, and returns the `Binary` with a resolvable `url` (`:2597-2609`). **[partially confirmed — BB-R-009; the two Medplum-specific affordances are new]**
27. `401` (never 403) for an expired or invalid bearer token (`:3727`). **[new]**
28. If a `RateLimit` header is emitted at all, it is `"name";r=N;t=S` — malformed values **throw** in `rateLimitStatus()` (`:3888`, `:3896`). And a reset longer than 2 s silently disables all retries (`:3828`). **[new — uncovered]**

**Phase 4 — the one admin route.**

29. `POST {baseUrl}admin/projects/{id}/invite` accepting the `InviteRequest` shape and returning **200** with the `ProjectMembership` (`:4623-4625`, `:612-637`). It is the only admin route `@medplum/core` types. **[confirmed — D2, T5]**

**Phase 5 — optional, only if the app opts in.**

30. `202` + `Content-Location` + a poll-able status URL for `Prefer: respond-async` (`:3759-3765`, `:4909-4934`). **[confirmed — D4, ADM-004]**
31. `/ws/subscriptions-r4` + `Subscription/{id}/$get-ws-binding-token` → `Parameters{token, websocket-url, expiration}`. **[confirmed — BB-R-025]**

**Not required for SDK-grade C** (contrary to a natural reading of "runs unmodified"): every `/auth/*` `Login`-protocol route, FHIRcast, CDS Hooks, `email/v1/send`, `keyvalue/v1/*`, `$expand-profile`, the GraphQL `StructureDefinitionList`/`SearchParameterList` connections, `Agent/$push`, `Bot/$execute`, `Patient/$summary`, `$graph`. These are methods the client *offers*; a program that does not call them does not need them.

**Items Big Book's current bricks do not cover** (checklist numbers): **7** (token lifetime vs grace period), **15** (JSON content-type on every response), **16** (CORS with credentials), **20** (`SUBSETTED` tagging), **21** (`_offset` on history), **26** partially (`_filename`, `X-Security-Context`), **28** (`RateLimit` header format and its retry side-effect). Plus the two new slugs in **18**.

##### (b) Proposed Java SDK method list for BB-R-012

Grouped, mapped onto BB-R-012's eight sub-items. **v0.1** unless marked.

**§1 Auth** — `loginWithClientCredentials(clientId, secret)` [SDK-017]; `loginWithPassword(user, pass)` *(Keycloak ROPC, dev-only — **no MedplumClient equivalent**, see below)*; `exchangeAuthorizationCode(code, verifier, redirectUri)` [SDK-021]; `startPkce()` [SDK-097]; `loopbackAuthorizationCodeLogin(...)` *(the JVM answer to `signInWithRedirect`)*; `refreshIfExpired()` [SDC-003]; `logout()` [SDK-034]; `setAccessToken` / `getAccessToken` / `isAuthenticated` / `clear` [SDK-023/024/042/047]; `getProfile()` / `getProfileAsync()` / `refreshProfile()` [SDK-036/037]; `getProject()` / `getProjectMembership()` / `isSuperAdmin()` / `isProjectAdmin()` [SDK-038/039/044/045]. **v0.2:** `loginWithClientAssertion(jwt)` [SDK-018]; `exchangeExternalToken(...)` [SDK-020]; `setBasicAuth(...)` [SDK-022]; `getUserConfiguration()` / `getAccessPolicy()` [SDK-040/041].

**§2 Typed CRUD** — `create(T)` [064]; `createIfNoneExist(T, query)` [065]; `read(Class, id)` [048]; `readReference(Reference)` [049]; `vread(Class, id, versionId)` [050]; `history(Class, id, count, offset)` [051]; `update(T)` [066]; `patch(Class, id, List<PatchOperation>)` [068]; `delete(Class, id)` [069]; `upsert(T, query)` [067]. **v0.2:** `readCanonical(Class, url)` [052].

**§3 Search** — `search(Class, Map)` [058]; `searchOne(Class, Map)` → `Optional<T>` [059]; `searchResources(Class, Map)` → `ResourceList<T>` carrying `getBundle()` [060]; `searchPages(Class, Map)` → `Iterator<List<T>>` — **this is the `next()` BB-R-012.3 promises** [061]; `fhirSearchUrl(Class, Map)` [011]. `_include` helpers are a query-builder concern: add `SearchBuilder.include("Observation:subject")` — **MedplumClient has no `_include` helper at all**, it is just a query param.

**§4 Batch / GraphQL / validate** — `executeBatch(Bundle)` and `executeTransaction(Bundle)` [071]; `graphql(query, operationName, variables)` [072]; `validate(IBaseResource)` [070].

**§5 Binary** — `createBinary(InputStream, contentType, filename)` [074]; `createAttachment(...)` [075]; `download(String urlOrBinaryRef)` → `InputStream` [057]. **v0.2:** `createMedia(...)` [078]; `createDocumentReference(...)` [079].

**§6 Admin helpers** — `inviteUser(projectId, InviteRequest)` [090]. **v0.2:** `createClient(projectId, ClientApplicationRequest)` — **no MedplumClient equivalent**, the app posts to `ADM-007` directly; `createProject(name)` — **no MedplumClient equivalent**, the app uses `/auth/newproject` which is non-goal, so Big Book's must be a BB-only route.

**§7 Spring Boot starter** — `BigBookAutoConfiguration` binding `bigbook.url` / `bigbook.client-id` / `bigbook.client-secret` / `bigbook.extended-mode` / `bigbook.refresh-grace-period` → a `BigBookClient` bean; plus a `BigBookClientCustomizer` hook and the `resourceModified` listener interface (SDC-011).

**§8 Autobatching** — non-goal. See (c).

**Not in §1–7 but MedplumClient has it, and Big Book should:**
- `get`/`post`/`put`/`patch`/`delete` **raw escape hatches** [012–016] and `wrappedFetch` [100]. Every real SDK needs a hole to punch through; add to §2.
- `startAsyncRequest` + `pollStatus` [081, SDC-007]. Needed by BB-R-020 `$export`; **v0.2**.
- `rateLimitStatus()` [098]. Pairs with BB-R-023; **v0.2**.
- `fhirUrl(...)` [011] — trivially useful, currently implied only by §2.
- `patientEverything(id)` [053] — **v0.2**; the single most-used Medplum operation not in BB-R-012.
- `executeBot(...)` [083] — **v0.2**, belongs with BB-R-016.
- `subscribeToCriteria(...)` [092] — **v0.2**, belongs with BB-R-025.

**Things BB-R-012 promises that have no MedplumClient equivalent:**
- **§1 "password (dev only)"** — `MedplumClient.startLogin` is the `Login`-protocol route, *not* an OAuth2 `password` grant. Big Book's version is a Keycloak ROPC call; it is a new method, not a port. Wire-compat is not at stake because no Medplum client uses ROPC.
- **§3 "`_include` helpers"** — no such helper exists; it is a query-param convention.
- **§6 `createClient` and `createProject`** — no client method; `createProject` in Medplum only exists through the non-goal `/auth/newproject`.
- **§7 Spring Boot starter** — obviously no analogue.

##### (c) Auto-batching: is the non-goal safe?

**What it actually does** (SDC-001, `client.ts:1363-1379`, `:3942-3994`): it is a **client-side read coalescer**, off by default (`autoBatchTime` default 0, `:1100-1106`). When on, `GET`s under the FHIR base are held for `autoBatchTime` ms and, if two or more accumulate, packed into a `Bundle{type:'batch'}` and POSTed to the FHIR base. It only ever batches `GET`s — the `AutoBatchEntry.method` type is the literal `'GET'` (`:803`). Writes are never batched (`post`/`put`/`patch`/`delete` go straight to `request()`). Responses are unpacked **positionally**, `entry[i]` ↔ caller `i`.

**Does any server behaviour depend on it?** No. It is purely a request-shape optimisation on the client side of the wire. The server sees an ordinary batch Bundle it must already support for `executeBatch` (checklist item 23). Two second-order requirements it *adds* to that support:
1. entries must come back **in request order** (no `fullUrl` correlation is attempted);
2. a failed entry must carry an `OperationOutcome` in `entry.response.outcome` whose `id` slug is not one of `ok`/`created`/`not-modified`/`accepted`, or the client resolves the caller with `undefined` instead of rejecting (`:3988-3991`).

Both are already implied by item 23 for `executeBatch`. Nothing is new.

**Is the non-goal safe? Yes** — and the reasoning in BB-R-012.8 ("server-side batches are enough") is correct but incomplete. Three supporting facts worth recording:
- It is **opt-in and off by default**, so `@medplum/core` itself does not exercise it unless an app sets `autoBatchTime`. It cannot break SDK-grade C.
- Its purpose is to rescue **React render storms** — dozens of independent `useResource()` hooks firing `readResource` in the same tick. A Java service does not have that shape; it batches by writing a batch.
- The JVM has a better tool anyway: virtual threads / `CompletableFuture.allOf` give real parallelism, where JS had only a single event loop to coalesce on.

One caveat for the docs: because a JVM client will *not* auto-batch, the server's per-request overhead is more exposed. If Baymax's read path turns out to be N+1-shaped, the answer is `executeBatch` or `_include`, not resurrecting this.

##### (d) What a Java SDK cannot reasonably reproduce

| MedplumClient mechanism | Why it does not port | JVM-idiomatic equivalent |
|---|---|---|
| `localStorage`-backed `ClientStorage` (SDC-008) | No browser origin; and writing refresh tokens to disk by default is a worse security posture on a server than in a sandboxed browser origin | A `TokenStore` SPI: `InMemoryTokenStore` (default), `FileTokenStore` (opt-in, 0600, for the CLI in BB-R-017), `VaultTokenStore` (`full` profile). Never persist unless asked. |
| Web Locks cross-tab refresh (SDC-003) | No sibling tabs; `navigator.locks` has no analogue | Keep the **single-flight** semantics with an `AtomicReference<CompletableFuture<Void>>` inside the client instance. Cross-*process* coordination is out of scope: two JVMs sharing one refresh token is a deployment mistake, not a client feature. |
| `window` `storage` event listener (SDC-009) | Same | Nothing. Drop it. |
| `EventTarget` / `TypedEventTarget` (SDC-011) | A DOM idiom | A small `BigBookClientListener` interface (`onResourceModified`, `onUnauthenticated`, `onOffline`) plus, in the Spring starter, `ApplicationEventPublisher` events. Keep `resourceModified`; drop `profileRefreshing`/`storageInitialized`. |
| PKCE stored in `localStorage` across a redirect (SDK-097, `:4166-4184`) | There is no page unload to survive; and hiding the verifier in client state is what forced the storage dependency | Return the verifier to the caller: `PkceChallenge startPkce()` → `{challenge, method, verifier}`, and take it back as a parameter in `exchangeAuthorizationCode`. Stateless and testable. |
| `signInWithRedirect` / `signOutWithRedirect` / `signInWithExternalAuth` (SDK-030/031/032) | They call `location.assign` | For desktop/CLI (which is what BB-R-012.1 actually means by "desktop/mobile"): a **loopback redirect** helper — bind `http://127.0.0.1:{ephemeral}`, open the system browser via `java.awt.Desktop`, capture `?code`, call `exchangeAuthorizationCode`. This is RFC 8252 and is the right shape. For a server-side web app: hand the authorize URL to the app's own framework; the SDK should build the URL, not navigate. |
| `XMLHttpRequest` upload-with-progress (SDK-076) | XHR is browser-only; and it bypasses the whole retry/refresh pipeline | If progress is wanted, a counting `HttpRequest.BodyPublisher` wrapper — but keep it inside the normal request pipeline, unlike Medplum. |
| `createPdf` via pdfmake (SDK-077) | A JS document-definition DSL | Drop. The app produces bytes with OpenPDF/iText and calls `createBinary`. |
| `ReadablePromise` (used as a return type throughout) | It exists so React Suspense can synchronously `read()` a resolved promise | Blocking `T` returns, with an optional `CompletableFuture<T>` mirror for the async-minded. Do not invent a Java `ReadablePromise`. |
| The browser request cache defaulting to 60 s (SDC-002) | A JVM service caching FHIR reads for 60 s by default would be a correctness hazard, not a convenience | Default **off**, matching Medplum's own Node default (`DEFAULT_NODE_CACHE_TIME = 0`, `:119`). Offer an opt-in Caffeine cache. |
| `fetch` injection (`MedplumClientOptions.fetch`) | — | This one *does* port: accept a `java.net.http.HttpClient` (or an OkHttp/`RestClient` adapter) so callers can wire proxies, TLS, and timeouts. Keep it. |

---

#### Contradictions with REQUIREMENTS.md / ADRs

**C1 — BB-R-012.1: "authorization code + PKCE (desktop/mobile)".**
> "`BigBookClient` with auth: client credentials, password (dev only), authorization code + PKCE (desktop/mobile), token refresh, `getProfile()` = `/auth/me`."

Code reality: three separate corrections. (i) **"password (dev only)"** has no MedplumClient counterpart — `startLogin` (`client.ts:1540`) is the Medplum `Login`-protocol route, not an OAuth2 `password` grant, and ADR-003 keeps that route non-goal; Big Book's password login is a Keycloak ROPC call and is a Big Book invention, which is fine but should be labelled as such rather than implying a Medplum port. (ii) **PKCE state**: `startPkce` (`:4166-4184`) writes `pkceState` and `codeVerifier` into `ClientStorage`; a Java SDK must return the verifier to the caller instead (see (d)). (iii) **"`getProfile()` = `/auth/me`"** is not quite right: `getProfile()` (`:3346`) is a pure in-memory getter; the call is made by `refreshProfile()` (`:3246-3269`) and awaited by `getProfileAsync()` (`:3356`). Minor, but the brick reads as if `getProfile()` performs I/O.

**C2 — BB-R-012.2: `upsert(search)` is understated.**
> "Typed CRUD on HAPI R4 structures: `create/read/vread/update/patch/delete/upsert(search)`."

Code reality: `upsertResource` (`:2415-2442`) is a FHIR **conditional update** (`PUT /{type}?{query}`), which additionally requires the server to return `412` with `OperationOutcome.id = "multiple-matches"` on an ambiguous match (`outcomes.ts:195-207`, `getStatus` maps `multiple-matches` → 412 at `:479-481`). That slug is **not** in `medplum-parity.md`'s recorded list, nor in ADR-003's consequence line ("OperationOutcome mapping, 409→412 on `If-Match`"). HAPI returns `412` for ambiguous conditional updates but with no `id`, so `isConflict`/`getStatus` misclassify it as 400. Add `multiple-matches` to the D4/BB-R-014.6 glue list.

**C3 — BB-R-012.3: `next()` and `_include` helpers.**
> "`search(Class, params)` returning a page with `next()`; `searchOne`; `searchResources` (unwrapped); `_include` helpers."

Code reality: the paging analogue is `searchResourcePages` (`:1923-1945`), an async generator that forces `_count=1000` and follows `Bundle.link[relation='next']`. Two implications the brick does not state: the server **must** emit an absolute `next` link, and `_count=1000` must be accepted — BB-R-014.6 caps `_count` at 1000, so the cap has to be inclusive or every paged search fails on the first page. Separately, **`_include` helpers do not exist in MedplumClient**; `_include` is passed as a plain query parameter. The brick is promising something Medplum does not have, which is fine, but it is a Big Book addition not a port.

**C4 — BB-R-012.4: `graphql(query)` vs what the SDK actually needs GraphQL for.**
> "`executeBatch/Transaction`; `graphql(query)`; `validate`."

Code reality: all three port cleanly (SDK-071/072/070). But note `validateResource` (`:2913`) POSTs a **bare resource**, not a `Parameters` wrapper — worth a line in the parity guide. And the *interesting* GraphQL dependency is elsewhere: `requestSchema` (`:2081-2147`) issues a GraphQL query over `StructureDefinitionList(_filter: "name eq X")` and `SearchParameterList(base: "X", _count: 100)`, which HAPI's GraphQL provider does not expose. No Big Book brick records this divergence. It does not block SDK-grade C (nothing in a headless `MedplumClient` flow calls `requestSchema`) but it **does** block `@medplum/react`, and therefore should be recorded against the v1.0 app-grade line in ADR-003 rather than discovered later.

**C5 — BB-R-012.5: Binary is missing two Medplum-specific affordances.**
> "Binary: `createBinary(InputStream, contentType)`, `download(url)`."

Code reality: `createBinary` (`:2597-2609`) sends `?_filename={name}` as a query param and `X-Security-Context: {reference}` as a header. Neither is standard FHIR and neither is HAPI behaviour. BB-R-009 covers Binary storage but mentions neither; BB-R-009.4 defers "binary security context" to v0.2 without noting that the *header spelling* is what the SDK sends. Also `download` (`:3391`) accepts a bare `Binary/{id}` string and only attaches `Authorization` for internal URLs (`isInternalUrl`, `:4005-4021`) — so BB-R-009.3's presigned URLs must work **without** a bearer header, which is consistent, but the `isInternalUrl` path-prefix rule (same origin *and* under the baseUrl path) is a constraint on where MinIO/presigned hosts may live and is not recorded anywhere.

**C6 — BB-R-014.6 "422→400 on validation" contradicts `getStatus()`.**
> "v0.2 glue: OperationOutcome `id`/`issue.code` mapping, 409→412 on `If-Match`, **422→400 on validation**, `X-Trace-Id`."

Code reality: `outcomes.ts` has a `business-rule` slug (`:25`, `businessRule()` at `:392`) that `getStatus()` maps to **422** (`:502-503`). So Medplum does emit 422 for business-rule failures, and the SDK understands it. The "422→400" rule presumably comes from HAPI emitting 422 for *validation* failures where Medplum emits 400 (`badRequest`, no `id` → `getStatus` default 400). Both are true but the brick states only half of it, and as written it would also rewrite a legitimate `business-rule` 422 into a 400. Recommended wording: "HAPI validation 422 → Medplum 400 (`OperationOutcome` with no `id`); preserve 422 only for `OperationOutcome.id = 'business-rule'`."

**C7 — BB-R-012.8 "Autobatching — non-goal (server-side batches are enough)".**
Not a contradiction. Verified in (c): auto-batching is an opt-in client-side read coalescer, off by default, and no server behaviour depends on it beyond ordinary batch-Bundle support. The non-goal is safe. One wording tweak: "server-side batches are enough" is right for the *write* path but the mechanism is actually about *reads*; suggest "client-side read coalescing — a React-render concern; the JVM answer is parallel calls or an explicit batch."

**C8 — BB-R-014.5 / ADR-003's SDK-grade exit test does not prove SDK-grade compat.**
> BB-R-014 exit test (v0.2) and ADR-003's decision table both read: "`@medplum/core` `MedplumClient` with `baseUrl` pointed at Big Book completes client-credentials login, `/auth/me`, and a Patient create/read/search without patching."

Code reality: the test as written **would pass on a server that fails at least six checklist items in (a)**, because a client-credentials + create/read/search script never exercises them:

| Not exercised | Proof line | Consequence of failing it |
|---|---|---|
| `login_id` claim | `jwt.ts:87-94` | Actually *is* exercised — `/auth/me` is in the test. Good. |
| Error-slug mapping | `outcomes.ts:437-534` | A happy-path script never sees a 4xx. `isGone`/`isNotFound`/`isConflict` silently always-false. |
| `_offset` on `_history` | `client.ts:2228` | Paged history silently returns page 1 forever. |
| `Bundle.link` `next` + `_count=1000` | `:1933-1943` | `searchResourcePages` returns one page and stops. |
| `SUBSETTED` tagging | `:3688` | A `_summary` search poisons the read cache with truncated resources. |
| Conditional create / update (`If-None-Exist`, `PUT ?query`) | `:2392`, `:2421` | `createResourceIfNoneExist` and `upsertResource` fail. |
| 401-on-expired-token | `:3727` | A 403 instead of 401 turns a recoverable refresh into a hard failure. |
| Token lifetime ≥ grace period | `:3211-3216` | A short-lived script never notices; a long-running one refreshes on every call. |
| CORS with credentials | `:4047-4049` | Node-based tests never notice; a browser app fails entirely. |

Recommended exit test (replace both BB-R-014.5's v0.2 line and ADR-003's proof cell):

> An unmodified `@medplum/core` script against Big Book must, in one run: (1) complete `startClientLogin`; (2) have `getProfile()`, `getProject()`, `getProjectMembership()` and `getAccessPolicy()` all non-undefined after login; (3) `createResource` a Patient and get the body back with a server-assigned id; (4) `createResourceIfNoneExist` the same Patient and get the **existing** one; (5) `upsertResource` it by identifier; (6) `readResource` it, then `deleteResource` it, then `readResource` again and assert the thrown `OperationOutcomeError` satisfies `isGone(err.outcome)`; (7) `readResource('Patient','00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000')` and assert `isNotFound`; (8) create 1001 Patients and assert `searchResourcePages` yields two pages; (9) `readHistory` with `{offset:1}` and assert a different page; (10) run the whole script from a browser against a different origin.

Items 6, 7 and 10 are what turn a smoke test into a compat test.

**C9 — ADR-003 Consequences: the `/oauth2/token` wrapper is understated.**
> "Token issuer is the Big Book public URL; `/.well-known/openid-configuration` and `/oauth2/logout` are re-shaped by Big Book, everything else proxied to Keycloak unchanged (BB-R-004 §8)."

Code reality: `/oauth2/token` **cannot** be proxied unchanged at SDK-grade. `verifyTokens` (`:4708-4738`) reads `tokens.project` and `tokens.profile` from the response **body**, and `isMedplumAccessToken` (`jwt.ts:87`) requires a `login_id` **claim**. Area 1 **D3** already recommends wrapping the token endpoint in v0.2; ADR-003's consequence line still says "everything else proxied unchanged" and should be amended to name `/oauth2/token` alongside `/oauth2/logout` as re-shaped at SDK-grade.

**C10 — ADR-003 Options: "SDK-grade C — `@medplum/core` `MedplumClient` runs unmodified" is ambiguous.**
Code reality: `MedplumClient` has ~100 public methods (Table A), of which ~35 are `skip` on the Big Book side — `Login`-protocol auth, FHIRcast, CDS Hooks, key-value, `$expand-profile`, GraphQL schema introspection, Agent push, PDF. "Runs unmodified" cannot mean "every method works"; it means "a program that uses the supported subset runs unpatched". Recommend ADR-003 say so explicitly and point at this area's Table A `Java SDK disposition` column as the definition of the subset. Otherwise the v0.2 gate is unfalsifiable.

---

#### Decisions needed

**D** = design call (changes a brick or an ADR line). **T** = text fix (brick wording is wrong about Medplum; no design change). Numbering continues the global sequence (D42/T42).

##### Design calls

| # | Topic | What Medplum does | What the docs say now | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D42 | Access-token lifetime vs `refreshGracePeriod` (no brick) | `isAuthenticated()` is false once the token is within 300 s of expiry (`client.ts:3211-3216`, default `:120`); `refreshIfExpired` then fires on **every** request | Nothing | Set the Keycloak realm access-token lifespan to **≥ 15 min** in the shipped realm import, and document `refreshGracePeriod` as the tuning knob. Add to BB-R-004 as a §8 sub-item. One config line, not code. |
| D43 | CORS with credentials (no brick) | Every internal request sets `credentials: 'include'` (`:4047-4049`) | Nothing; BB-R-011/BB-R-014 are silent | Traefik/Spring CORS must echo the request origin and set `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true` — never `*`. Required for any browser app, including the v0.1 Appsmith overlay if it calls Big Book directly. Add to BB-R-014.6 as v0.1 config (it costs nothing). |
| D44 | `SUBSETTED` tagging on narrowed responses (no brick) | `cacheResource` refuses to cache a resource tagged `SUBSETTED` (`:3688`); without the tag, a `_summary`/`_elements` result becomes the cached "full" read | Nothing | HAPI does tag `SUBSETTED` on `_summary`/`_elements` by default. **Verify and pin it** rather than assume. Add one line to BB-R-002 and one assertion to the exit test. No code. |
| D45 | `_offset` on `_history` (BB-R-002) | `readHistory` sends `_count` and **`_offset`** (`:2224-2229`) | BB-R-002 does not name the history paging params | HAPI uses `_getpagesoffset`/`_count` with opaque paging links on `_history`. Decide: (a) accept `_offset` as a synonym on history (~15 lines of glue, v0.2), or (b) declare `readHistory({offset})` unsupported and rely on `next` links. Recommend **(a)** — it is trivial and `readHistory` is on the SDK-grade happy path for any audit UI. |
| D46 | Two missing `OperationOutcome.id` slugs (BB-R-014.6, `medplum-parity.md`) | `multiple-matches` → 412 (ambiguous conditional update) and `business-rule` → 422 (`outcomes.ts:195-207`, `:392-405`, `getStatus` `:471-510`) | `medplum-parity.md` records the slug requirement but not these two; BB-R-014.6 says "422→400 on validation" flat | Add both slugs to the v0.2 mapping table, and reword the 422 rule per **C6**: HAPI validation 422 → 400; preserve 422 only for `business-rule`. |
| D47 | `RateLimit` header format and its retry side-effect (BB-R-023) | `rateLimitStatus()` **throws** on a malformed header (`:3888`, `:3896`); a reset longer than `maxRetryTime` (2 s default) **disables retries entirely** (`:3828`) | BB-R-023 says "Rate limits per project/user — wire Traefik" | If Big Book emits `RateLimit` at all it must be exactly `"name";r=N;t=S`. Traefik's rate-limit middleware does not emit this header by default — so either add a tiny response filter emitting the Medplum spelling, or **emit nothing** (the SDK handles absence gracefully). Recommend **emit nothing in v0.2**; revisit with BB-R-023. |
| D48 | `Binary` upload extensions (`_filename`, `X-Security-Context`) (BB-R-009) | `createBinary` sends `?_filename=` and `X-Security-Context:` (`:2597-2609`) | BB-R-009.1 covers raw POST + content type; 009.4 defers security context without naming the header | Accept and honour `?_filename=` in v0.1 (map to `Binary.contentType`/`DocumentReference.content.attachment.title` convention — a few lines). Record `X-Security-Context` as the exact v0.2 spelling in BB-R-009.4. |
| D49 | ADR-003's SDK-grade exit test (BB-R-014.5, ADR-003 proof table) | See **C8** — the current test passes on a server failing ≥6 contract items | "completes client-credentials login, `/auth/me`, and a Patient create/read/search without patching" | Replace with the 10-step script in **C8**. It is still one script and one CI job; it just asserts the things that actually break. |
| D50 | "Runs unmodified" needs a defined subset (ADR-003 Options) | ~35 of ~100 public methods are out of scope for Big Book (Table A) | ADR-003: "SDK-grade C — `@medplum/core` `MedplumClient` runs unmodified" | Amend ADR-003 to define the supported subset by reference to this area's Table A `Java SDK disposition` column, and list the out-of-scope method families inline (Login-protocol auth, FHIRcast, CDS Hooks, keyvalue, schema introspection, Agent push, PDF). Otherwise v0.2's gate is unfalsifiable. |
| D51 | `/oauth2/token` is re-shaped, not proxied (ADR-003 Consequences) | Token **body** must carry `project`/`profile`; token **claims** must carry `login_id`, `exp`, `cid`/`client_id` (`:4708-4738`, `jwt.ts:87`) | ADR-003: "`/.well-known/openid-configuration` and `/oauth2/logout` are re-shaped …, everything else proxied to Keycloak unchanged" | Amend the consequence line to name `/oauth2/token` as re-shaped **from v0.2**. This is the same call as area 1 **D3**; recording it here so the ADR text is fixed in the same pass. |
| D52 | BB-R-012 scope additions (BB-R-012) | MedplumClient has raw HTTP escape hatches, `startAsyncRequest`, `rateLimitStatus`, `patientEverything`, `executeBot`, `subscribeToCriteria` | BB-R-012's eight sub-items omit all six | Add to BB-R-012: **§2a** raw `get/post/put/patch/delete` + `fhirUrl` (v0.1 — every SDK needs an escape hatch); **§9** `startAsyncRequest`/`bulkExport` (v0.2, with BB-R-020); **§10** `rateLimitStatus` (v0.2, with BB-R-023); **§11** `patientEverything` (v0.2); **§12** `executeBot` (v0.2, with BB-R-016); **§13** `subscribeToCriteria` (v0.2, with BB-R-025). |

##### Text fixes (apply on bless, no design change)

| # | Brick | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| T42 | BB-R-012.1 "password (dev only)" | Not a Medplum port. `MedplumClient.startLogin` is the `Login`-protocol route (`client.ts:1540`), which ADR-003 keeps non-goal. Big Book's password login is a Keycloak ROPC call and is a Big Book addition. Reword: "password (Keycloak ROPC, dev only — **not** a Medplum-compatible flow)". |
| T43 | BB-R-012.1 "`getProfile()` = `/auth/me`" | `getProfile()` (`:3346`) is an in-memory getter. The call is `refreshProfile()` (`:3246`), awaited via `getProfileAsync()` (`:3356`). Reword: "`refreshProfile()`/`getProfileAsync()` = `GET /auth/me`; `getProfile()` returns the cached session". |
| T44 | BB-R-012.3 "`_include` helpers" | No such helper exists in `MedplumClient`; `_include` is a plain query parameter. Keep the Big Book helper if wanted, but drop the implication that it is a port. |
| T45 | BB-R-012.3 "returning a page with `next()`" | The Medplum analogue is `searchResourcePages` (`:1923-1945`), an async generator. It forces `_count=1000` when unset and follows `Bundle.link[relation='next']` — both are server requirements. Add: "requires the server to emit an absolute `next` link and to accept `_count=1000` (the BB-R-014.6 cap must be **inclusive**)". |
| T46 | BB-R-012.4 "`validate`" | `validateResource` (`:2913`) POSTs a **bare resource**, not a `Parameters` wrapper. HAPI accepts both, so no glue is needed — but record it in `medplum-parity.md` so nobody "fixes" it later. |
| T47 | BB-R-012.6 "`createClient`, `createProject` (super-admin)" | Neither has a `MedplumClient` equivalent. `createClient` corresponds to `ADM-007` (posted directly by `@medplum/app`), and `createProject` in Medplum only exists via `/auth/newproject`, which is non-goal. Label both **BB-only** so they are not mistaken for compat surface. `inviteUser` is the only one `@medplum/core` types (`:4623`). |
| T48 | BB-R-012.8 "Autobatching — non-goal (server-side batches are enough)" | Accurate conclusion, imprecise reason. Auto-batching coalesces **reads**, not writes, is opt-in (`autoBatchTime` default 0, `:1100`), and exists for React render storms. Reword: "client-side read coalescing — a React-render concern; the JVM answer is parallel calls or an explicit batch". |
| T49 | BB-R-014.5 "v0.2 = `@medplum/core` runs unmodified (SDK-grade)" | Cross-reference the checklist. Add: "where 'unmodified' means the supported method subset defined in the Area 5 inventory; the `Login`-protocol auth methods, FHIRcast, CDS Hooks, key-value, schema introspection, Agent push and PDF helpers are out of scope." (Pairs with D50.) |
| T50 | ADR-003 Open #2 / `medplum-parity.md` headline divergences | Two additions to the recorded slug divergences: `multiple-matches` (412, ambiguous conditional update) and `business-rule` (422). Also record that `getStatus()` defaults an **unrecognised** `id` to **400** (or 500 when `issue[0].code === 'exception'`, `outcomes.ts:508`) — which is why a HAPI error with no `id` reads as 400 to every SDK helper. |
| T51 | BB-R-009 (Binary) | `createBinary` (`:2597-2609`) sends `?_filename=` (query) and `X-Security-Context:` (header). Record both spellings; `_filename` is v0.1-cheap, the security context stays the v0.2 deferral already in 009.4. Also: `download` (`:3391`) attaches `Authorization` only for URLs that are same-origin **and** under the `baseUrl` path (`isInternalUrl`, `:4005-4021`) — a constraint on where presigned/MinIO hosts may live. |
| T52 | BB-R-002 / `medplum-parity.md` | `readHistory` sends `_offset` (`:2228`), not `_getpagesoffset`. Record the divergence even if D45 chooses to alias it. |
| T53 | v1.0 app-grade line (ADR-003 / REQUIREMENTS.md v1.0 section) | Add the GraphQL schema-introspection divergence: `requestSchema` (`:2081-2147`) queries `StructureDefinitionList(_filter: "name eq X")` and `SearchParameterList(base: "X", _count: 100)`, and `requestProfileSchema` (`:2157-2194`) calls `StructureDefinition/$expand-profile`. Neither exists in HAPI. Harmless for SDK-grade (no headless flow calls them), blocking for `@medplum/react`/app-grade. |

## Area 6 — workers, subscriptions, WebSocket (draft)

### 6.1 Queues, subscription delivery, and the WebSocket surface


Source snapshot: medplum `packages/server/src` (`workers/*`, `ws/*`, `bots/*`, `pubsub.ts`, `redis.ts`, `heartbeat.ts`, `server-registry.ts`, `fhir/precommit.ts`, `util/auditevent.ts`, `fhir/operations/getwsbindingtoken.ts`, `fhir/repo.ts`) plus `packages/core/src/subscriptions/index.ts`. Docs snapshot: `docs/medplum-docs` scraped 2026-09-12. `Source` paths are relative to `packages/server/src` unless prefixed `core/`. BB-R mapping is against REQUIREMENTS.md at big-book commit `bbdf237`.

Conventions used in Behaviour: "queue" = a BullMQ `Queue` backed by Redis; "attempt" counts `job.attemptsMade` (0-based on the first run); `defaultQueueOptions` (`workers/utils.ts:389-400`) gives every queue `attempts: 3`, `backoff: {type:'exponential', delay:1000}` unless a worker overrides them. All workers share one Redis connection config (`backgroundJobsRedis ?? redis`, `workers/utils.ts:385-387`).

---

#### Area 6 — decisions needed

**D** = design call (changes a brick or an ADR line). **T** = text fix (brick wording is wrong about Medplum; no design change). Numbering continues the global sequence (D53+, T54+).

##### Design calls

| # | Topic | What Medplum does | What the docs say now | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D53 | **ADR-002 (event bus) is not needed for anything in v0.1–v0.2** | Medplum's "event bus" is BullMQ-on-Redis: a durable job queue with per-job retry/backoff, not a broker. Nothing in the subscription, cron, download, or bot path is pub/sub between services; the only true pub/sub (`pubsub.ts`) exists solely to fan a WebSocket event out to whichever HTTP node holds the socket — a multi-node concern that `lite` (one JVM) does not have. | ADR-002 "Kafka vs RabbitMQ vs none in `lite`"; "requirements blocked on this: BB-R-007 (v0.2 extras), BB-R-016" | **Decide ADR-002 = none, in both profiles, for v0.1–v0.2.** HAPI's `SubscriptionMatcherInterceptor` + `SubscriptionDeliveryQueue` already gives an in-JVM delivery queue; Spring `@Scheduled` + a Quartz/ShedLock-backed DB table covers cron and retries. Revisit only if (a) Big Book ever runs >1 JVM *and* needs WebSocket subscriptions (BB-R-025), or (b) BB-R-016 wants bots on separate worker nodes. Neither is v0.1/v0.2 scope. Unblocks BB-R-007 and BB-R-016 today. |
| D54 | **Retry numbers in BB-R-007.3 are unspecified; Medplum's are extreme** | Two layers. BullMQ `attempts` is set to `MAX_JOB_ATTEMPTS = 19` on the queue, but the *effective* cap is `subscription-max-attempts` (default `DEFAULT_ATTEMPTS = 4`) enforced in `catchJobError`; on exceeding it the worker throws `UnrecoverableError` so BullMQ stops. Backoff is custom `cappedExponential`: `min(20_000 * 2^(attemptsMade-1) * jitter, 8h)`, jitter uniform in [0.9, 1.1]. Server-side errors (loading the Subscription/resource, "the preamble") get a separate, much larger budget: up to 19 attempts, then the job is silently dropped. | BB-R-007.3 says only "Retry with backoff; configurable max attempts" | **Pin the numbers in the brick:** default 4 attempts total, base delay 20s, exponential ×2, cap 8h, ±10% jitter, honour `subscription-max-attempts`. Also adopt the **preamble/delivery split** (infrastructure errors retry longer than the subscriber's endpoint failing) — HAPI has no equivalent and it is ~20 lines. |
| D55 | **Rest-hook retries are not resumed across restart in `lite`** | BullMQ persists every job in Redis, so a delayed retry survives a server restart and is picked up by any node. A `@Scheduled` in-JVM retry (the obvious Spring answer) loses in-flight retries on restart unless the retry state is in Postgres. | No brick says whether delivery is durable | **Make the delivery queue a Postgres table**, not an in-memory `ScheduledExecutorService`: `subscription_delivery(id, subscription_id, resource_type, resource_id, version_id, interaction, attempt, next_attempt_at, status)` polled every second by a single `@Scheduled` with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`. ~150 lines, keeps the 5s exit test, survives restart, and is the same table a second JVM would share. Note: HAPI's own `SubscriptionDeliveryQueue` is **in-memory (LinkedBlockingQueue) by default** — a HAPI restart drops pending deliveries — so this is glue Big Book must write regardless of "wire HAPI". |
| D56 | **`X-Signature` HMAC is over the body only, no timestamp, no replay defence** | `createHmac('sha256', secret).update(body).digest('hex')`, hex-encoded, sent as `X-Signature` (`workers/subscription.ts:855-860`). Secret is read from `subscription-secret` (with legacy fallback URL `…/StructureDefinition-subscriptionSecret`). No timestamp, no nonce, no version prefix, and no signature at all when the extension is absent. | BB-R-007.4 "Signature header (`X-Signature`, HMAC of body with a per-subscription secret)" | **Match the wire format byte-for-byte** (n8n/bot recipes in BB-R-008 depend on it) but **additionally** send `X-Bigbook-Timestamp` and accept a `v1=<hex>` form in a second header for new integrations. Document that `X-Signature` alone is replayable. Cheap; avoids shipping a known-weak scheme as the only option. |
| D57 | **Subscription access-policy enforcement is a no-op for rest-hooks** | `satisfiesAccessPolicy` (`workers/subscription.ts:236-308`) builds the subscription author's `AccessPolicy` and checks READ on the resource — then **`return subscription.channel.type === 'websocket' ? satisfied : true`**. For rest-hooks it logs a warning and delivers anyway. The `TODO` at `:228` admits this. | BB-R-007.1 "scoped to the project"; BB-R-006 says policies gate reads | **Do not inherit the bug.** In Big Book, a rest-hook Subscription delivers only what the author's `AccessPolicy` permits to read, enforced, from v0.1. This is a divergence from Medplum wire behaviour and must be stated in `medplum-parity.md`. It is also the cheapest place to enforce it (one `satisfiedAccessPolicy` call the policy adapter already has). |
| D58 | **`Bot.runAsUser` on the subscription path resolves to the resource *author*, not a caller** | `workers/subscription.ts:903-911`: `requester = resource.meta.author`; with `runAsUser` the bot runs under **the membership of whoever wrote the resource**, which may be an entirely different user from the Subscription's author. No cookie is forwarded on this path (there is no HTTP request). | Area 3 D26 covers `runAsUser` generally; no brick mentions the subscription-path resolution | **Fold into D26** as an explicit sub-case: on a subscription trigger, `runAsUser` = the triggering resource's author's membership. For BB-R-016, recommend **not implementing `runAsUser` on the subscription trigger at all in v0.2** — a Camel route triggered by a data change running as an arbitrary third party is a privilege-escalation shape with no good use case. `$execute` `runAsUser` (a real caller) can stay. |
| D59 | **Cron is a first-class resource in Medplum, and BB-R-016 only mentions it as a bot trigger** | Two schedulables: `Bot.cronString`/`Bot.cronTiming` (legacy, scheduler key = bare bot id) and the `Cron` resource (`cronString`, `active`, `endTime`, `targetReference`, `onBehalfOf`; scheduler key `Cron/<id>`). `Cron` carries its own identity (`onBehalfOf` membership, validated same-project) and the whole `Cron` resource is passed as the bot's input. Requires project feature `cron`. `reloadCronBots()` obliterates and re-registers everything. | BB-R-016 lists "triggers: subscription, cron, `$execute`" with no resource | **Adopt the `Cron` resource, drop `Bot.cronString`.** One schedulable type, identity explicit via `onBehalfOf`, `endTime` supported. Map to Quartz/ShedLock with the `Cron` resource id as the job key; re-register on startup by scanning `Cron?active=true`. Add to BB-R-016 as a named sub-item. |
| D60 | **`subscriptionAutoDisable` is Redis-only and would be lost in `lite`** | `subscription-failure-tracker.ts`: a Redis **sorted set** per subscription (`medplum:sub:failures:<id>`), score = epoch ms, pruned to the largest configured window, `ZCOUNT` per trigger. Triggers come from `Project.systemSetting[subscriptionAutoDisable]` (JSON array) falling back to server config `subscriptionAutoDisable: [{maxConsecutiveFailures, timeWindowSeconds}]`. On trip: an atomic `withTransaction` JSON-Patch (`test /status == active`, `replace /status = off`, `add /error = "Automatically disabled after N consecutive failed events in the last W seconds"`) plus a `SeriousFailure` AuditEvent. | No brick mentions auto-disable at all (GAP) | **Add auto-disable to BB-R-007 as v0.2** and implement the counter as a column on the delivery table (`consecutive_failures`, `first_failure_at`) rather than a sorted set — ADR-006 forbids Redis and a per-subscription counter does not need one. Keep the exact `Subscription.status = 'off'` + `Subscription.error` + AuditEvent wire behaviour. |
| D61 | **WebSocket subscriptions (BB-R-025) cannot be built the Medplum way without Redis** | The whole WS path is Redis: an active-subscription hash per `(project, resourceType)` (`pubsub.ts:25-27`), a per-user active set, a `Subscription/<id>` cache entry, a pub/sub channel `WEBSOCKET_SUB_PUBLISH_CHANNEL` that fans the event to the node holding the socket, and a 1h JWT from `$get-ws-binding-token`. | BB-R-025 "Subscription extras (WebSocket …) — wire/glue" | **For a single-JVM `lite`, none of it is needed**: an in-JVM `Map<subscriptionId, Set<WebSocketSession>>` plus Spring's `@ServerEndpoint`/`WebSocketHandler` reproduces the protocol exactly, and HAPI has a `websocket` channel type already. State in BB-R-025 that WS subscriptions are **`lite`-only single-node** until ADR-002 is revisited; a `full` multi-replica deployment needs either sticky sessions at Traefik or the broker. This is the one place ADR-002 could matter, and it is v0.2+. |
| D62 | **Medplum has ten more queues Big Book has no brick for** | `DispatchQueue`, `DownloadQueue`, `BatchQueue`, `ReindexQueue`, `SetAccountsQueue`, `PostDeployMigrationQueue`, `DataWarehouseSyncQueue`, `DicomQueue`, `LambdaCleanerQueue` (plus Subscription and Cron). Several are AWS-specific or Medplum-schema-specific. | Nothing | **Fates:** Dispatch → non-goal (an artefact of Medplum's sync→async migration; HAPI interceptors are already the dispatch point). Download → defer v0.3 (BB-R-009 GAP; see WRK-011). Batch → wire HAPI async `$transaction` + BB-R-020's AsyncJob machinery, v0.2. Reindex/SetAccounts/PostDeployMigration/LambdaCleaner → non-goal (already D8 in area 1). DataWarehouseSync → non-goal. Dicom → non-goal (explicit in REQUIREMENTS non-goals). |
| D63 | **Pre-commit bot subscriptions are the only synchronous hook, and they are a validation/mutation hook** | `fhir/precommit.ts:41-146`: gated by server `preCommitSubscriptionsEnabled` **and** `Project.setting[preCommitSubscriptionsEnabled]`; searches active Subscriptions carrying the `pre-commit-bot` extension, matches the same criteria matcher, runs the bot **inline in the write transaction**, and **the bot's return value replaces the resource being written**. A non-success result aborts the write with the bot's `OperationOutcome`. Only the **first** matching pre-commit subscription runs. The normal async path skips these (`workers/subscription.ts:369-372`). | Nothing (GAP) | **Defer to v0.3, do not promise it.** The Big Book equivalent is a HAPI `STORAGE_PRESTORAGE_RESOURCE_CREATED/UPDATED` interceptor calling a Camel route synchronously — feasible, but a bot that can rewrite or reject every write is a large blast radius and it is not on any roadmap line. Record as a named non-goal for v0.1/v0.2 so it does not get smuggled into BB-R-016. |

##### Text fixes (area 6)

| # | Brick | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| T54 | BB-R-007.2 "Interaction filter extension (`create`-only / `update`-only / `delete`) — HAPI supports; map the Medplum extension URL" | The URL is `https://medplum.com/fhir/StructureDefinition/subscription-supported-interaction`, `valueCode`, and it is **repeatable** — a Subscription may carry several, and the interaction matches if *any* one matches (`core/subscriptions/index.ts:762-779`). Absent ⇒ all three interactions fire. Note also that HAPI's equivalent is `SubscriptionTopic`/`R5` shaped, not this extension, so this is **glue over HAPI**, not "HAPI supports". |
| T55 | BB-R-007.1 "fires on create/update/delete matching the criteria" | Incomplete. Delivery is additionally suppressed by: `Subscription.status !== 'active'`; the resource being an `AuditEvent` (never fires, `workers/subscription.ts:337-340`); the resource being a websocket `Subscription` itself (`:346-348`); a **`meta.account` intersection test** — if the Subscription carries accounts, at least one must match the resource's (`core/subscriptions/index.ts:785-801`); and the rest-hook endpoint failing the outbound-URL allowlist (`isAllowedOutboundUrlForQueue`). Add the account rule at least; it is a real multi-tenant filter. |
| T56 | BB-R-007.4 "`X-Signature`, HMAC of body with a per-subscription secret" | Two extension URLs are accepted: `https://www.medplum.com/fhir/StructureDefinition/subscription-secret` (current) and `https://www.medplum.com/fhir/StructureDefinition-subscriptionSecret` (legacy, note `www.` and the `-` instead of `/`). Both take `valueString`. Encoding is lowercase **hex**, not base64. |
| T57 | BB-R-007.5 "`$resend` operation to re-fire a resource's subscriptions — **glue** (thin)" | It is not an operation; it is `POST /fhir/R4/:type/:id/$resend` routed to `Repository.resendSubscriptions` (`fhir/repo.ts:1425-1456`, route `fhir/routes.ts:450`). Requires **project admin or super admin**. Body options: `{interaction?: 'create'|'update'|'delete', subscription?: '<ref>', verbose?: boolean}`, default `interaction: 'update'`. On `update` it re-reads history and **412 preconditionFailed** if the resource is not at its latest version. It re-evaluates criteria (it does not blindly re-send). "thin" is right; add the 412 and the admin gate. |
| T58 | BB-R-007.3 "delivery status visible as one `AuditEvent` per delivery attempt" | Correct, and the shape is: `type.code = 'transmit'`, `source.observer = Subscription/<id>`, `entity` = [resource, subscription, (bot)], `period.start/end`, `outcome` = `0` (Success) or `4` (MinorFailure), `outcomeDesc` = `` `Attempt <n> received status <code>` `` or `` `Attempt <n> received error <err>` ``, `meta.project/account/accounts` copied from the Subscription. **Destination is configurable** via repeatable `https://medplum.com/fhir/StructureDefinition/subscription-audit-event-destination` `valueCode ∈ {resource, log}`, default `resource`. That is BB-R-007.7's "AuditEvent as destination / log-only" — it is the *destination of the delivery AuditEvent*, **not** "AuditEvent as a subscription channel type". Reword BB-R-007.7. |
| T59 | BB-R-007.6 "Expression-based criteria (FHIRPath `%previous` vs `%current`) — HAPI `in-memory` matcher gap" | The URL is `https://medplum.com/fhir/StructureDefinition/fhir-path-criteria-expression`, `valueString`, evaluated with `%current` = the new resource and `%previous` = the prior version (or `{}` when absent, e.g. on create) (`core/subscriptions/index.ts:832-850`). It runs **before** the search-criteria match, so an expression that throws or returns false short-circuits. On the pre-commit path `%previous` is always `{}` (`fhir/precommit.ts:103`). |
| T60 | BB-R-008.3 "Signature verification snippet for the webhook node" | The n8n recipe also needs `X-Medplum-Subscription` (the Subscription id) and `X-Medplum-Interaction` (`create`/`update`/`delete`), and on delete `X-Medplum-Deleted-Resource: <Type>/<id>` with an **empty JSON body `{}`** — a delete rest-hook does *not* carry the resource. Custom headers come from `Subscription.channel.header[]`, each entry a literal `"Key: value"` string split on the first `:`. Add all four to the recipe or the example workflow breaks on deletes. |
| T61 | BB-R-016 "triggers: subscription, cron, `$execute`" | Medplum has seven bot invocation surfaces; area 3 lists them. From the worker side the two relevant extras are the **`Agent` WebSocket transmit** path (`ws/agent.ts:248-257`, HL7v2 in, bot out — that is BB-R-028/v0.3) and **pre-commit** (D63). Also: a bot triggered by a subscription receives the **rest-hook headers** (`X-Medplum-Subscription`, `X-Medplum-Interaction`, `X-Signature`) as its `headers` input, so a Camel route must expose them. |
| T62 | BB-R-016 "Bot SDK (Camel …)" — timeout and secrets | Medplum bot timeout: `Bot.timeout` seconds, default **10s** for `vmcontext` (`bots/vmcontext.ts:48,106`) and taken from the Lambda function config for `awslambda` (default 10s). Secrets are injected as a `Record<string, ProjectSetting>` merged from up to four sources in order bot-project-system → bot-project → runAs-project-system → runAs-project, later winning (`bots/utils.ts:282-308`); `Project.systemSecret` is only included when `Bot.system === true`. A Camel-based BB-R-016 must reproduce the timeout and the merge order or shared/linked-project bots misbehave. |

---

#### 6.1 Background workers and queue infrastructure

| ID | Capability | Source (file:line) | Behaviour | Doc ref | Used by | Proposed fate | BB-R |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| WRK-001 | Worker registry and lifecycle | `workers/index.ts:21-63`; `workers/utils.ts:131-192` | Eleven workers are declared in a static `workerDefs` array and initialised in order: dispatch, subscription, download, cron, reindex, batch, post-deploy-migration, set-accounts, lambda-cleaner, data-warehouse-sync, dicom. `config.workers.enabled` selects which run on this host (`'*'` or omitted = all; `[]` = an HTTP-only pool that still owns the queues but runs no worker). Each initializer returns `{queue, worker, name}`; the queue is registered even when the worker is disabled, so any node can enqueue. Startup **fails hard** if `setGlobalConcurrency`/`removeGlobalConcurrency` cannot be applied. `closeWorkers()` closes workers before queues, in reverse registration order, so a draining job can still enqueue its successor. | `self-hosting/server-config.md — Deploy & Operate > Self-Hosting > Configuring > Server Config` | none (server-internal) | glue — Spring `@Scheduled` + `TaskExecutor` beans, no registry needed | BB-R-007, BB-R-016 |
| WRK-002 | BullMQ + Redis as the queue substrate | `workers/utils.ts:385-400`; `redis.ts:44-75` | Every queue is a BullMQ `Queue` over ioredis. Five logical Redis roles exist (`default`, `cache`, `rateLimit`, `pubSub`, `backgroundJobs`), each optionally a distinct instance, falling back to `default`. Queue defaults: `attempts: 3`, exponential backoff from 1000ms. Per-worker BullMQ options merge in increasing precedence: global `bullmq` config → worker-code defaults → `workers.bullmq.<name>`. Jobs, their retry schedule, delayed state and repeat schedulers are **all persisted in Redis**, so a restart resumes them and any node can pick them up. `reconnectOnError` retries on `READONLY`/`LOADING` (failover). | `self-hosting/server-config.md — Deploy & Operate > Self-Hosting > Configuring > Server Config` | none | **non-goal** — ADR-006 forbids Redis in `lite`; replace with a Postgres-backed table (D55) | GAP (ADR-002) |
| WRK-003 | `queueRegistry` + global concurrency | `workers/utils.ts:141-192`, `306-316` | In-process registry keyed by queue name; rejects duplicate registration. `globalConcurrency` is a **queue-level property stored in Redis**, cluster-wide, so it must be set imperatively at startup rather than via constructor options; omitting it clears any previously-set limit. Per-worker `concurrency` (local) is separate. | none | none | non-goal — one JVM, use a bounded `ThreadPoolTaskExecutor` | GAP |
| WRK-004 | Job metrics wrapper `trackJobMetrics` | `workers/utils.ts:272-290` | Wraps every processor to maintain `inFlightJobs` (up-down counter) and `jobsCompleted`. Deliberately not using BullMQ `active`/`completed`/`failed` events because they do not pair up (a job moved to delayed suppresses `failed`; both terminal events are suppressed once the connection closes), which would strand the in-flight gauge above zero. Keeps three declared parameters because BullMQ introspects `processor.length >= 3` to decide whether to pass an abort signal. | `self-hosting/monitoring.md — Deploy & Operate > Self-Hosting > Monitoring` | none | glue — Micrometer `@Timed`/`Counter` on the delivery poller | BB-R-015 |
| WRK-005 | `DispatchQueue` — the async fan-out point | `workers/dispatch.ts:41-193` | One job per resource change carrying only `{interaction, resourceType, id, versionId, previousVersionId?, requestId?, traceId?}` — **no resource body**; the worker re-reads both versions via `systemRepo.readVersion`. Queue defaults (3 attempts, 1s exponential). Gated by `config.dispatchEnabled`. From here it calls, in order and each inside its own try/catch so one failure cannot suppress the others: `addSubscriptionJobs`, `addCronJobs` (also on delete — a deleted resource has a schedule to tear down), then on non-delete `addDownloadJobs` and, for `DicomInstance`, `addDicomJobs`. Also deletes the AWS Lambda when a `awslambda` Bot is deleted. Historically this work was synchronous in the request path; moving it here is what allows dedicated worker hosts. | none | none | **non-goal** — HAPI's `STORAGE_PRECOMMIT_RESOURCE_*` interceptors are already the dispatch point; no second hop needed | BB-R-007 |
| WRK-006 | `SubscriptionQueue` — declaration and backoff strategy | `workers/subscription.ts:148-211` | Queue `SubscriptionQueue`, job name `SubscriptionJobData`, `attempts: MAX_JOB_ATTEMPTS = 19` with a **custom** `cappedExponential` backoff registered on the worker: `min(BASE_DELAY(20_000) * 2^(attemptsMade-1) * jitter, MAX_DELAY(8h))` where `jitter ∈ [0.9, 1.1]`. 19 attempts ≈ 72–80 hours wall clock. The worker runs the processor inside `runInAuthenticatedContext(job.data.authState, …)` when the enqueuing request was authenticated, else `tryRunInRequestContext`, so the original `requestId`/`traceId`/auth follow the job. Emits `queuedDuration` (first attempt only), `executionDuration`, `totalDuration`, `failedExecutionDuration` histograms. Concurrency is whatever `workers.bullmq.subscription.concurrency` says — **unset by default, i.e. BullMQ's default of 1 per worker process**. | `subscriptions/index.md — Automate > Subscriptions` | none | glue — Postgres `subscription_delivery` table + `@Scheduled` poller, same numbers (D54, D55) | BB-R-007.3 |
| WRK-007 | `CronQueue` — repeatable job schedulers | `workers/cron.ts:43-166`, `400-441` | Uses BullMQ **job schedulers** (`upsertJobScheduler(key, {pattern}, {data})`), not ordinary jobs. Scheduler key is the bare resource id for a `Bot` (legacy; changing it would orphan every registered scheduler) and `Cron/<id>` for a `Cron`. Registration is idempotent and **only fires when the effective cron string changed** (old vs new compared before touching the queue). Requires `Project.features` to contain `cron`; on delete the scheduler is removed *without* consulting features (a deleted resource can never run). `Bot.cronTiming` (a FHIR `Timing`) is converted to a 5-field cron by `convertTimingToCron`, clamped to at most once per minute. `reloadCronBots()` **obliterates the whole queue with `force: true`** and re-registers every `Bot` with a cron and every active `Cron` by paging with a 1000ms inter-page delay. | `bots/bot-cron-job.md — Automate > Bots > Bot Triggers & Invocation > Cron Jobs for Bots` | `examples/` cron bots; app Bot editor | glue — Quartz (or Spring `@Scheduled` + ShedLock) keyed on `Cron.id` | BB-R-016 (D59) |

Losing the project `cron` feature **skips the tick but keeps the schedule** (the feature can come back and nothing would re-register it). `onBehalfOf` belonging to another project **throws** rather than running. Body `{interaction?, subscription?, verbose?}`, default `interaction: 'update'`. For `update` it reads the last two history entries, **412 preconditionFailed** if the head version has moved, and passes the prior version as `%previous`. It then calls `addSubscriptionJobs` with the same context — so criteria are **re-evaluated**, not bypassed; `options.subscription` narrows to one Subscription reference. Creates no new version and no history entry. | `api/fhir/operations/ — Reference > API > FHIR > Operations` | `MedplumClient`; app resource page | glue (thin) — one controller over the same matcher | BB-R-007.5 (T57) |
| SUB-021 | Delivery ordering and concurrency | `workers/subscription.ts:153-185`; `workers/utils.ts:306-316` | There is **no ordering guarantee**. Jobs for the same resource are independent BullMQ jobs; with `concurrency > 1` (or several worker hosts) two versions of one resource can be delivered out of order, and a failed v1 retrying 20 seconds later lands after a successful v2. The only mitigation is the stale-version check (SUB-012), which is applied **on retries only** — the first attempt for a superseded version still delivers. Delivery is therefore **at-least-once, unordered**: a job that succeeds at the HTTP layer but whose ack is lost, or a worker that dies after the POST, retries the POST. No idempotency key is sent (`X-Medplum-Subscription` + the resource's `versionId` inside the body is the closest thing). | none | none | glue — **document at-least-once**; optionally add a per-`(subscription, resource)` serialisation in the poller, which the 5s exit test does not need | BB-R-007 · GAP |
